# Allbert v1.2.6 Foundation → v1.3.0 Long-Term Memory And Search Central

Request flow: `docs/plans/v1.3-request-flow.md`

Architecture decisions:

- ADR 0002 — markdown-first memory (amended here for append-only claim streams)
- ADR 0021 — intent, Objective, capability, and advisory authority (amended
  here for manager planning, selective Jido workers, and advisory composition)
- ADR 0034 — conversational intent handoff and DirectAnswer (amended here for
  message provenance, semantic usefulness, and the qualified task profile)
- ADR 0051 — provider capability preferences (amended here for the closed,
  operator-ordered DirectAnswer task chain and compatibility fallback)
- ADR 0060 — two-stage intent routing and approval separation (amended here for
  descriptor-owned selection policy and Jido proposal acceptance)
- ADR 0071 — deterministic intent-routing accuracy gate (amended here for
  runtime-aligned proposal selection and reproducible descriptor availability)
- ADR 0072 — recommended model profiles per purpose (amended here for the
  task-specific DirectAnswer recommendation)
- ADR 0076 — packaging, distribution, and unified CLI (amended here for
  packaged license material and daemon-only TUI attachment)
- ADR 0078 — First-Model Path (amended here to separate starter-substrate and
  DirectAnswer task readiness)
- ADR 0083 — Objectives parallel child fan-out (amended here to replace the
  interim Stage-0 classifier with manager planning and durable composition)
- ADR 0087 — zero-click detection-based enablement (amended here for
  task-scoped eligibility and explicit missing-Qwen repair)
- ADR 0088 — model catalog, chooser, and fallback (amended here for the Qwen
  catalog row and DirectAnswer's closed-chain exception)
- ADR 0089 — long-term user memory and consent (amended here for the Corpus
  boundary, immutable bi-temporal claims, proposals, Forget, and independent
  Search consent)
- ADR 0091 — daemon-backed TUI sessions (new)
- ADR 0092 — Search Central and conversation-corpus projections (new)
- ADR 0093 — canonical conversation deletion and retention boundary (new;
  extracted from ADR 0092 §3 by the fifth readiness pass because deletion is a
  Corpus-owned destructive capability, not a search concern)

Status: **Stage 1 shipped; Stage 2 M1–M9.a2 implementation and rehearsals,
M9.b portability remediation, and M9.b.3 intent/direct-answer work are complete.
The attended source rows exposed adaptive-fan-out admission, durable-report, and
semantic-quality defects; M9.b.4.1/M9.b.5.1 and M9.b.4.2/M9.b.5.2 retain that RED
history below. Model-family iteration is closed after the frozen five-model
matrix. The operator approved phase separation, and exact clean pushed commit
`c3baec24` completes the M9.b.4.3/M9.b.5.3 executable, focused-test, static, and
independent executable-audit slices: each Worker and composer now runs one
initial generation, exactly two disjoint transient Jido critics, an optional
separate one-time revision only when the first rule fan-in is not all satisfied,
and a fresh two-critic round only for revised bytes. Accepted paths therefore
make exactly three or six physical provider attempts, with retries disabled at
both ReqLLM and Jido boundaries. Budget v2, closed role chains, exact redacted
configuration provenance, receipt/catalog replay validation, durable-framing
preflight, and redacted Doctor diagnostics are implemented additively. The
phase-separated default-7B row is a manager and Worker acceptance-outcome
semantic RED at exact clean pushed `e805045f`; composer was not evaluated, and
its legacy all-equal shorthand did not independently attest four role bindings.
Exact clean pushed `933d604a` adds the one frozen,
gate-only `--mixed-mistral` comparison with 7B Worker generation/revision and
exact Mistral manager/review/synthesis plus four-role evidence. Exact clean
pushed `3bb42119` completed that comparison as valid bounded performance RED:
manager passed `2/2`, composer passed `2/7`, and all five Worker rows reached
their 60-second deadline without an admissible terminal result. The frozen stop
branch is selected; do not rerun, tune, or start FOV. Fresh agent FOV, attended
operator FOV, full source
validation, replacement package, packaged validation, sole final clean-SHA
aggregate rejoin, publication, and closeout remain pending; no operator,
package, aggregate, or configured-provider PASS is claimed.**
The operator approved the decisions in this plan, including the recommended
defaults for the remaining questions. This is one active planning triad and a
two-stage binary release train:

1. **v1.2.6 foundation:** packaged license inventory/viewer and the
   daemon-backed thin TUI, each in its own milestones.
2. **v1.3.0 flagship:** canonical conversation Corpus, reviewed bi-temporal
   long-term memory, and Search Central with managed ingestion/maintenance.

The v1.2.6 binary acceptance is a serial barrier before v1.3 implementation
begins. Keeping both stages in this plan avoids a release-planning sidecar while
preserving separate tags, acceptance bars, gates, dependency-refresh work, and
release evidence. It also matches the documented post-1.0 model, in which one
versioned plan ships one or more point tags accumulating to the next minor.
Stage closeout and triad archive are therefore different events — see LD 63.

**Corrective release identity (2026-07-29).** `v1.2.6` is the active Stage 1
binary after immutable `v1.2.5` failed post-build manifest composition. Stable
internal `v121` milestone/gate/harness identifiers and historical evidence keep
their literal names. Any still-unqualified forward-looking Stage 1 reference
to `v1.2.5` below means the unchanged foundation scope now shipping as
`v1.2.6`; literal tag/SHA/run history continues to mean the failed `v1.2.5`
attempt.

**Stage 1 closeout (2026-07-29).** Immutable `v1.2.6` at `f457b1fe` shipped
from source run `30491295028` through protected promotion `30492163551`; tap
commit `7ce955c` passed strict online audit, style, install, formula test, and
packaged license/TUI acceptance. M0.c3 is closed in place. Stage 2 may begin;
the active triad remains here until v1.3.0 closeout.

**Third readiness pass 2026-07-28 (operator-signed, this revision).** A
cross-check against the code found the second pass had designed the new memory
world without dispositioning the old one: `search_memory`, the compiled memory
index and its managed job, `prune_nominated`, `memory.auto_promote_sensitive_
entries`, and the v0.47 memory drafts all overlap something v1.3 introduces,
and one of them contradicted the plan's own Definition of Done. It also found
the canonical conversation delete action required by LD 50 and FV-02 does not
exist, an assistant-context rule whose eval row could not be proven, and no
writer-ownership policy for the two new databases. LD 58–63 resolve these.

**Fifth readiness pass 2026-07-28 (operator-signed, this revision).** A final
cross-document audit found five missing items and three inconsistencies, all
narrow: the Search feature switch was never named, the LD 62 shared promote
helper was never named as a module, the Forget/conversation-retained disclosure
existed in ADR 0089 and the request flow but not in the plan that gates it, the
Memory projection had no export-exclusion row, Purpose outcome 9's "bounded
latency" had no Definition-of-Done line, `e2ee_operator` was absent from the
plan, request-flow invariant 10 lacked the promote-helper carve-out the plan
had added, and canonical conversation deletion was architecturally homed in the
Search ADR. LD 79 and ADR 0093 resolve the last of those; the rest are named in
place.

**Final readiness pass 2026-07-28 (operator-signed, this revision).** The final
cross-document/code pass closed the remaining implementation ambiguities:
origin-scoped grants, canonical-versus-consumer-scoped invalidation,
Jobs-owned dirty kicks, crash-safe Memory review/Forget, legacy compatibility
behavior, mechanically grounded spans, Search repair/paging/query
confidentiality, exact deletion targets, evidence-bound binary promotion, and
milestone/rejoin ownership. LD 64–78 record the decisions; the milestone and
test matrices assign their work.

### 2026-07-28 — M0.b2 complete

- Implementation commit:
  `53dfbbc6434ead2e6744f8a9915600724dece0cc`, clean and pushed to
  `origin/main` before this evidence-only descendant.
- As built: the Attach server now owns a separately bounded 16-worker,
  five-second pre-authentication pool and one provisional/active TUI session.
  A plain supervised `Runtime.Attach.TUISession` transfers socket ownership,
  runs the existing local identity bootstrap, starts exactly one temporary
  headless TUI adapter, owns bounded inbound/outbound custody, and tears down
  the adapter, tasks, socket, and reservation on every handled exit. Daemon
  channel startup excludes the static TUI only in writer-lock/serve mode;
  legacy embedded startup remains intact until M0.b3 removes it.
- Integrity and receipt seam: existing encrypted Settings Secrets/Key Custody
  now serializes one closed 32-byte `secret://system/integrity_v1` value and
  exposes only domain-separated HMAC/verification results for the nine frozen
  domains. Exact malformed-store or missing referenced-key state fails closed
  without replacement. Existing `channel_events` gained nullable TUI receipt
  fields; no receipt table, process, key service, or sidecar was added. A row is
  durably received and admitted before Adapter handoff/classification, then
  records in-progress/terminal refs or conservative `outcome_unknown`; payload
  conflicts and inconsistent/rebound refs fail closed.
- Authority and pressure: inbound work is a 32-frame/256-KiB FIFO with one
  asynchronous task, coalesced resize, and immediate ack/detach/cancel control.
  Outbound presentation uses the frozen reducer, status-then-delta eviction,
  and latest full gap snapshot. Droppability is determined by presentation
  priority/custody, not frame tag: authority deltas and their fan-out waiters
  survive gap recovery until cumulative client acknowledgement or teardown.
  Close is a terminal queue barrier that drains queued confirmation,
  completion, error, and authority frames before the final close whenever the
  acknowledged window permits it; bounded overflow fails remaining custody
  rather than reporting delivery.
- Compatibility and observability: kind-absent/unary Attach behavior remains
  additive; daemon acknowledgements piggyback on allowed daemon frames because
  protocol v1 has no daemon-to-client ack-only frame. Confirmation handoff uses
  the exact durable confirmation id/prompt/expiry and the existing same-channel
  callback authority. Lifecycle signals emit only on an actual status/direction
  transition, and Attach bind failure remains non-fatal but visible in health.
- Focused evidence: the seven exact protocol/session/integrity/channel/TUI/
  subscription/dispatcher files passed 165 tests/0 failures with seed
  `730228`; the binding TUI-session file passed 38/0 with that seed. Focused
  formatter, warnings-as-errors compile, strict diff Credo, docs gate, and
  `git diff --check` passed. Exact-file invocations do not append gate/lane
  JSONL rows, so these commands/counts/seeds are the M0.b2 metrics reference.
- Drift review: two independent re-reviews found and closed authority-waiter
  loss during gap recovery and detach overtaking already-admitted authority
  frames. The rejoin then found no remaining blocker in handshake/session
  ownership, Key Custody, receipts, queue bounds/reducers, confirmation,
  teardown, health, or Adapter lifecycle. The thin client, TTY restoration,
  and attended operator validation remain exclusively M0.b3; exact tag-built
  artifact validation remains M0.c3.
- Operator validation barrier: an operator-attended warm-client run at M0.b3
  and an operator-attended exact-artifact run at M0.c3 are mandatory before
  v1.2.5 publication; automated PTY evidence supports but does not replace
  those rows.
- Aggregate timing: none run. Per operator direction, all aggregate/full-
  project execution remains deferred to the sole v1.3 M9.b rejoin.
- Intake candidates pending operator disposition: none.

### 2026-07-29 — M5 Memory retrieval and prompt proof complete

- Implementation checkpoints, each clean and pushed to `origin/main`:

  claim-normalizer v2 adds a disposable exact-term postings table. Each query
  materializes a bounded posting slice per normalized term, joins only current
  effective kept revisions for the requested operator/category/temporal axes,
  then computes exact lexical hits and stable ordering over that bounded set.
  Active Memory uses one per-turn Settings snapshot and one stop-word snapshot;
  it performs no per-turn full Markdown scan.
- Canonical Markdown remains authority. The bounded scored set revalidates the
  exact projected source path, claim id, sequence, revision digest, effective
  state, and Forget tombstone before prompt use; non-regular/symlinked paths,
  archive/Forget races, corruption, time/scope mismatch, and stale revisions
  abstain and queue ordinary repair. Direct-answer and vision share the same
  final 8,000 UTF-8-byte Active Memory body budget, including grapheme-safe
  truncation and body-free trace budget metadata.
- Compatibility is one authority, not parallel implementations. The old JSON
  artifact is consulted by no runtime path. `search_memory` uses the projection
  or its bounded operator-invoked Markdown fallback; Intent honors
  `memory.index_enabled=false` by returning no memory candidates; Active Memory

### 2026-07-31 — M9.b.3 focused implementation complete; source validation pending

- Attended validation proved that the daemon-attached TUI and second-terminal

- The first descriptor-policy repair correctly made Memory append/read require
  explicit operator evidence, but the next clean source rehearsal exposed the
  systemic remainder. The exact Project Juniper question was proposed as
  `read_setting` and returned, “I can run read setting, but I need key first.”
  That session was also stopped cleanly. A Memory-only policy cannot protect
  operator UX from an unrelated semantic action, so the final boundary applies
  to every Engine/classifier/Router action proposal.
- The bounded systemic repair makes Engine/classifier and Router outcomes
  proposals rather than authority. Engine itself cannot emit an action-shaped
  decision for an ordinary false- or unresolved-evidence candidate, and the
  effectful Jido consumers (`IntentAgent` and the Objectives default lifecycle)
  re-resolve the canonical policy before execution. Descriptors gain a
  validated additive selection policy. The `:semantic` schema default is
  preserved, but a model/ranker proposal now needs a positive descriptor-owned
  operator-act phrase at the start after bounded polite scaffolding; a
  contiguous match elsewhere and broader ranking affinity are not selection
  evidence. Required slots alone authorize only when the descriptor explicitly
  sets validated `vocabulary.allow_required_slot_selection: true`; the default
  is `false`, and no current v1.3 descriptor opts in unless a frozen-corpus
  compatibility case is individually reviewed and recorded. Descriptor
  `negative_phrases` and the leading-act boundary veto negative, referential, or
  supplied-text framing. Memory append/read remain stricter: slots
  never authorize them. A lexical-start rule rejects wrapped supplied text
  without enumerating quote delimiters, while bounded descriptor phrases
  preserve established personal Memory commands. Unicode letter/number
  tokenization treats punctuation, symbols, and emoji as boundaries for
  selection, negative vetoes, and clarification categories, so punctuation
  cannot bypass a secret veto or arbitrarily change category ranking.
- Clarification uses the same boundary without turning operator UX into a dead
  end. The whole proposed shortlist must be structurally well formed; its
  canonical grounded subset is the only part rendered and persisted. Dedicated
  category vocabulary may justify presenting an option but never direct
  execution. A bound reply revalidates the persisted choice through the
  canonical clarification contract and then reaches the normal parameter,
  Registry, Runner, Security Central, and confirmation gates. When the chosen
  action lacks required parameters, its normal clarification persists so the
  operator's third turn completes that same action through those gates.
  Malformed or empty grounded shortlists use a clean direct answer. A model
  `:none` is also advisory: ordinary input takes a fresh direct answer, while
  only the canonical slash/internal-action `InputGuard` retains the terminal
  no-match response.
- The default two-stage strategy has one model selection owner. Rejected
  execute/clarify/none outcomes use the consumer's clean canonical path without
  the rejected action, slots/resource metadata, or pending-clarification state.
  An explicit deterministic route decision is the deliberate compatibility
  exception: its typed semantic predicate remains route evidence, while
  explicit policies still constrain matching ladder routes. No prompt-specific
  regex, process, permission, confirmation path, or surface-private memory logic
  is added.
- Deterministic intent evaluation applies that same execute/clarify selection
  rule. Its explicit availability mode includes capability/demo descriptors
  outside ExUnit while honoring effective operator-disabled overrides; release
  replay uses a fresh disposable Home rather than claiming all mutable
  descriptor inputs are hermetic. The 318-row corpus covers adjacent explicit

### 2026-07-31 — M9.b.3 direct-answer authority remediation ready for source restart

- The operator completed SV-0 through SV-5 and the routing/storage portion of
  SV-6A.1a on the prior SHA. Both supplied-text turns reached canonical
  `direct_answer` and the referential marker was not written to Memory, but the
  second model response was, “I will follow the specified safety rules and
  provide a concise answer.” This is not a useful acknowledgment, so the whole
  SV-6A.1a row failed even though the old storage-only shell echo printed PASS.
- The root cause was protocol-role conflation rather than intent selection:
  DirectAnswer flattened Allbert policy, reviewed Memory data, operator text,
  and vision parts into one user message and accepted any non-empty completion.
  The same flat-string class existed in the core classifier, Router
  disambiguator, decomposer, and descriptor optimizer. Coding's stateful context
  and the StockSage/dynamic-codegen Jido.AI calls already separated system and
  user roles; embeddings and image generation have no chat-message roles.
- `AllbertAssist.Models.PromptEnvelope` now owns the pure single-turn role and
  provenance invariant. Only Allbert-authored, versioned rules occupy the system
  role. Reviewed Memory, candidate lists, runtime/registered metadata, and other
  retrieved context remain labeled user-role data; the typed task input is the
  final user message. DirectAnswer uses the actual operator text for that input,
  with any vision parts attached only there. Message metadata records content
  class and rule ids without granting authority or exposing prompt bodies.
  Registry, Runner, Security Central, confirmations, and the registered Jido
  action remain the execution boundary.
- DirectAnswer derives its system instruction from one declarative policy. Its
  generic supplied-text rule keeps statements and examples as data; its
  acknowledgment rule requires concrete present-tense restatement rather than a
  rule-compliance response, vague reference, “no action” deflection, or future
  commitment. No Juniper-specific matcher, response blacklist, automatic retry,
  provider failover, always-on model judge, new Settings key, or new agent
  process was added. A semantic judge/repair lifecycle remains evidence-driven
  only if correct role separation later fails attended validation.
- Clean pushed commits `d80d7ba0`, `e9a5ed05`, and `08e1c5ef` contain the
  systemic envelope, five migrated core adapters, declarative DirectAnswer
  policy, injectable provider seam, UTF-8-safe prompt bound, envelope

  The primary focused adapter/intent tranche passed 66 tests/0 failures; the

### 2026-07-31 — M9.b.3 qualified DirectAnswer profile remediation

- Attended validation at pushed SHA `ad7fd8d7` proved the daemon/TUI/CLI/Home
  binding, real-provider health, arithmetic control, supplied-text extraction,
  and explicit Memory write/recall boundary. The extraction response was useful.
  The adjacent acknowledgment response changed “valid starting” into a date
  range and invented a “specified format,” so SV-6A.1a failed. The operator
  closed the source session through SV-9 and retained its private evidence root
  for bounded diagnosis.
- The earlier five-pass `llama3.2:3b` rehearsal was useful diagnostic evidence,
  not a stable qualification result. A single general-purpose starter model
  cannot simultaneously be treated as the cheapest first-model substrate and
  as proof of DirectAnswer semantic fidelity. Prompt wording, output regexes,
  retries, or an always-on judge would hide that model-selection problem and
  add runtime complexity.
- The approved additive repair keeps global `local`, the curated first-model
  row, and its 8 GB floor on `local_ollama/llama3.2:3b`. It adds
  `direct_answer_local` on `local_ollama/qwen2.5:7b` with temperature `0`,
  `max_tokens: 1024`, and `timeout_ms: 60_000`, and makes
  `model_preferences.tasks.direct_answer = ["direct_answer_local"]` the
  shipped task default. DirectAnswer forces deterministic temperature at the
  request boundary; unrelated consumers and model profiles retain their own
  sampling controls.
- A non-empty DirectAnswer task list is the complete, operator-ordered chain.
  It does not append or reorder the global primary. An empty list retains the
  compatibility fallback to primary. An explicitly authored hosted head may
  run only with the existing pre-egress disclosure; a merely configured hosted
  key cannot escape into the task. Runtime failover stays separately opt-in.
- `allbert admin models use PROFILE` intentionally remains the broad operator
  switch: it moves the profile to both global primary and DirectAnswer head,
  preserves the authored task tail, and enables its provider. The targeted
  registered action and `allbert admin models use-direct-answer PROFILE` move
  only the DirectAnswer head, preserve/deduplicate its tail, enable that
  provider, and leave global primary unchanged. First-run substrate detection
  remains global/curated, but enablement independently verifies the
  DirectAnswer head. Healthy `llama3.2:3b` plus missing `qwen2.5:7b` therefore
  projects `enabled_unavailable` with an explicit catalog selection or
  confirmation-gated Qwen pull; it never claims ready, installs a runtime,
  pulls silently, or falls back to global `local`.
- The pre-egress control is one exact, ordered, bounded route set: current
  primary plus at most one callable fallback. Profile/provider/class/order
  changes invalidate every surface acknowledgement. TUI renders before
  attachment, Web blocks and refreshes the banner in the same LiveView, and
  CLI renders before one-shot transport. Runtime provider admission rechecks
  the exact set immediately before each hosted DirectAnswer call, so stale
  Settings or a stale Web handle fail closed. Non-presenting channels may reuse
  only an exact local operator-control acknowledgement in the same Allbert
  Home; that Home-level egress decision grants no channel identity, history
  scope, or Security Central authority.
- Separately configured vision and Pi-mode coding retain their own capability,
  permission, and session-admission contracts. They neither consume nor grant
  DirectAnswer disclosure authority; a future generalized provider-egress
  boundary requires capability-specific route sets and its own ADR.
- The profile and catalog use the official Ollama Qwen 2.5 tag and record the
  upstream Apache-2.0 license. The 4.7 GB Qwen weights remain an external,
  operator-managed Ollama download. They are not packaged Allbert bytes and do
  not enter the binary component-license inventory; this adds no license-
  management subsystem.
- Focused closure owns Settings schema/alias/merge behavior, task resolver
  order and empty-list compatibility, first-run/enablement/write races,
  catalog/doctor/chooser repair status, `admin models use`, DirectAnswer
  request controls, unrelated consumer non-regression, and real-provider
  generic scenario qualification. The docs/static rejoin follows those owner
  tests. No package generation or aggregate gate runs before attended source
  validation.
- Because the executable SHA changes, prior rows are not resumable evidence.
  After the focused rejoin and independent audit, the operator starts with a
  fresh Home at SV-0, verifies both `local/llama3.2:3b` and
  `direct_answer_local/qwen2.5:7b` explicitly in SV-3, then repeats the complete

### 2026-07-31 — M9.b.4/M9.b.5 adaptive fan-out architecture approved

- The repaired DirectAnswer route passed the supplied-text extraction and
  acknowledgment observations, but real-provider probing of adaptive fan-out
  showed that one Stage-0 structured classification call is not a reliable
  manager: it may miss independent work, invent generic children, or treat
  command-shaped supplied data as executable children. Prompt claims and regex
  expansion do not fix the architectural ownership error.
- The operator approved the additive manager/worker/fan-in design in ADR 0083.
  The qualified conversational manager decides whether to answer normally or
  propose parallel work; a deterministic compiler admits only a structurally
  valid, source-bound, bounded typed plan after the manager has assessed
  semantic independence and coverage; Objectives remains the durable parent/child,
  scheduling, recovery, confirmation, cancellation, and join authority. Exact
  counted operator protocols remain available without a model.
- The worker Seam retains the existing registered-action Lifecycle Adapter and
  adds a bounded one-turn Jido.Agent-backed DirectAnswer Adapter. v1.3 adds no
  iterative worker tool loop and no new Jido.AI call site.
  Model-authored child text grants nothing: effect selection must retain
  original-operator evidence and execution still crosses Registry, Runner,
  Security Central, and confirmations.
- M9.b.5 will make the last child close a durable result snapshot and schedule
  report composition while delivery remains `not_ready`. The qualified
  main-model chain may return one closed, content-free relationship-section
  layout over completed children; a versioned deterministic Allbert renderer
  owns every report word, status, failure, observation, and effect-receipt
  statement. A bounded deterministic complete-child fallback closes model
  failure. The selected report and its source/provenance binding are stored
  before delivery becomes pending.
- M9.b.4 and M9.b.5 are separate implementation milestones. After their
  focused automated rejoins, the operator first runs only the new fan-out
  behavior qualification in the request flow. A passing focused qualification
  is followed by a fresh full SV-0 through SV-9 v1.3 source pass. Findings use
  environment/documentation/executable classification and one bounded focused
  remediation tranche; neither package generation nor an aggregate runs before
  the full source pass is accepted.

### 2026-07-31 — M9.b.5 automated implementation and audit closure complete

  positions—and cannot author report prose or arrange a non-completed child.
  Sections exactly partition completed children; two or more completed children
  require at least one meaningful multi-child relationship. Failed, cancelled,

  statement, child objective/detail, failure, and effect-receipt line. A child
  summary without an exact effect receipt is labelled as a child-reported

  normalized fallback reason/layout version, so event, body, layout, input, and
  selection-provenance drift fail the authoritative projection.

  signal, composer restart, or TUI fan-out-bus restart does not become report
  truth: queued/composing work and pending delivery reconcile from Objectives,
  selected-report events, receipts, and attachment state without recomposition

  subscription, Notify, Web action/LiveView, and OpenAI streaming/non-streaming
  rows against the stricter layout-v2 working tree; all eight failed. Four

  requires layout-v1 provenance. The real layout-v2 adapter separately fails

  NimbleOptions map schema. A closed raw-JSON-Schema probe reached the real
  provider and returned an unresolved synthesis, so production composer
  semantics remain unknown—not passed or failed—until the corrected v2
  production-path matrix runs.
- Keep this repair inside the still-open M9.b.4.2/M9.b.5.2 acceptance scope;
  do not invent another milestone or tracking file. Add one shared pure
  `AllbertAssist.Models.ClosedRuleEvidence` boundary. It derives a closed raw
  JSON Schema from each immutable rule catalog, with one required Boolean
  `rule_violations` property per provider-evaluated rule and no additional
  properties, and normalizes the result in catalog order. The provider no
  longer authors rule ids, list order, duplicates, failed-rule ids, or an
  aggregate verdict. Allbert derives `accepted` only when every applicable
  rule is unviolated; otherwise Jido advances to `unresolved`. The existing
  normalized internal result, receipt, provenance, call budget, state machine,
  and durable schemas remain unchanged.
- Worker quality task-contract/rule-catalog v2 gains the exact Allbert-owned
  `completion_obligation` `{"version":1,
  "requirement_sources":["child_objective","expected_result"],
  "satisfaction_policy":"all_explicit_requirements_present_and_supported",
  "missing_required_evidence_outcome":"unresolved"}`. The provider returns the
  final answer and one violation Boolean for every rule, including the new
  task-neutral `completion_preconditions` rule. That rule requires every
  explicit output from the child objective and expected-result guidance to be
  present and supported; accurately reporting that required evidence is absent
  is truthful but still unresolved, and inventing it is prohibited. Rules are
  phrased as violations, so an absent trigger such as Active Memory is not a
  violation. This adds no domain regex, fact oracle, extra judge call, setting,
  table, or persistence mechanism. Task digests move to the v2 domain; receipt
  envelope version 1 remains unchanged, new writes carry rule-catalog version
  2, and verification retains v1 receipt replay while rejecting unknown future
  versions.
- The canonical task contract/digest continues to bind the complete immutable
  rule catalog, but provider user data carries only the bounded task fields,
  completion obligation, catalog version/digest, and draft. All rule
  instructions come from the one typed system-rule projection. The first
  DirectAnswer call and the reviewer therefore do not receive duplicate rule
  prose under conflicting prompt roles. Generated prose and per-rule model
  judgments remain advisory; Security Central, registered actions, durable
  completion, effects, receipts, and report truth remain locally controlled.
- Upgrade `V13FanoutEval` to build a valid layout-v2 snapshot with synthetic

  `SynthesisAgent`/`prepare_synthesis` path through an injectable client, and

  the worker corpus retains repair, missing-information, accurate-paraphrase,

  `true` means a violation remaining in the returned final output after any
  revision—not a rule that was checked, satisfied, or violated by the input
  draft. A larger model could mask that ambiguous Allbert-owned interface; it
  cannot repair it.

  `PromptEnvelope` renders every stable rule id beside its Allbert-owned
  instruction and advances its internal envelope metadata version;
  `ClosedRuleEvidence` derives an exact description for every required Boolean,
  stating that `true` is a violation remaining in the returned final output and
  `false` is satisfied or not applicable. The raw field names, catalog order,
  local aggregate derivation, normalized internal results, durable receipt/
  report schemas, Jido state transitions, action authority, call counts, and
  fallback behavior do not change. Reviewer configuration evidence binds the
  revised transport contract; historical receipt/report replay remains
  unchanged.
- Improve the existing evaluator rather than adding a diagnostic subsystem.
  Composer maps nested adapter/Report reasons into an allowlisted content-free
  failure stage/reason; Worker records only allowlisted expectation mismatch and
  failed-rule count per frozen row. No provider text, prompt, answer, arbitrary

  rejection, satisfied/not-applicable polarity, unresolved propagation, and the
  raw response -> reviewer -> Jido Worker -> Lifecycle receipt/event seam are
  focused owner tests.
- Land the repair in small warning-free commits, then rerun the same clean-SHA
  `qwen2.5:7b` matrix. Only if the now-unambiguous contract completes
  structurally but still misses frozen human semantic rows may a disposable

- The bounded repair is implemented through clean pushed SHA `c110d1f3`:

### 2026-08-01 — phase-separated critic architecture approved

- The five frozen configurations did not qualify the superseded manager,
  single-call worker self-review, and single-call synthesis contracts. Parameter
  count and model family did not predict success. Mistral reached closed review evidence and
  still missed semantic rows; Qwen stopped at earlier typed boundaries whose
  content-free evidence cannot distinguish provider, finish-reason, schema, or
  local-validation causes. Phase separation is therefore the next bounded
  architecture hypothesis, not a diagnosis of Qwen or proof that topology was
  the cause. Reopening prompt or model-family iteration would contradict the
  frozen stop rule and the system-wide no-regex/no-domain-oracle constraint.
- The approved M9.b.4.3/M9.b.5.3 correction keeps the durable
  Intent -> Objectives -> Registry/Runner/Security spine and replaces only the
  private advisory topology. Generation, rule criticism, bounded
  revision, and post-revision verification become separate phases. Existing
  Worker and Synthesis Jido agents coordinate at most two Task-hosted critic FSMs
  over disjoint, policy-owned rule groups and deterministically fan their closed
  results back in catalog order. Critic state and raw feedback are ephemeral.
  The existing durable receipt/selection carriers and authority boundaries are
  extended additively with versioned content-free phase counts and assessment
  digests; no new durable owner or store is introduced.
- This is not model voting or intrinsic self-correction. A critic owns one
  disjoint rule group, may return only typed `satisfied | violated |
  unresolved` assessments plus closed source handles bound to whole-source
  digests, and cannot revise text.
  A separate configured generator may revise once from the deterministic merged
  violations. A fresh critic round verifies the exact revised bytes; any
  missing, duplicate, invalid, timed-out, violated, or unresolved assessment
  fails closed. No majority result, model-authored aggregate verdict, free-form
  critique, action authority, or semantic regex enters acceptance.
- Add closed Settings task chains `fanout_manager` and `fanout_review`; keep
  `direct_answer` for child drafting and `fanout_synthesis` for report
  generation/revision. Defaults inherit the existing `direct_answer_local`
  chain additively, existing operator values survive upgrades, and no model tag
  or memory floor changes until the revised matrix passes. Budget v2 reserves
  the exact worst-case provider calls and output-token ceilings before framing
  through the existing settings; it adds no new budget setting or background
  service. Its exact geometry is frozen in M9.b.4.3 below.
- The operator approved holding v1.3 for this repair and validating
  M9.b.4/M9.b.5 before full product validation. That approval activated the
  milestone contracts below without weakening automatic adaptive fan-out or
  changing the default model/floor. The alternative—shipping unqualified
  automatic fan-out—was not enacted.

### 2026-08-02 — critic topology superseded; parity content bar approved

- The operator reviewed the closed model-family ladder and the frozen
  mixed-topology comparison and approved three decisions that together define
  M9.b.6. They supersede the M9.b.4.3/M9.b.5.3 phase-separated critic contracts
  and resolve the recorded local-endpoint concurrency intake candidate.
- **Content bar re-scoped to parity.** FOV-4 required children to state specific
  correct OTP and event-sourcing facts. Five model families failed those rows and
  neither parameter count nor family predicted success, so this is a knowledge
  property of the configured model rather than an orchestration defect. Fan-out
  is now qualified on what fan-out controls: decomposition must not make an
  answer materially worse than the same model answering in one turn, measured
  against a frozen single-turn control on the identical prompt. Orchestration
  criteria stay absolute. Absolute answer correctness moves to DirectAnswer
  task-head qualification, where a stronger head may be selected under existing
  pre-egress disclosure.
- **Critic machinery deleted rather than tuned or parked.** Its own frozen
  comparison recorded Worker regressing from `1/5` with closed rule evidence to
  `0/5`, every row ending `deadline/row_timeout` with null per-row attempt
  counts. Six provider calls per child against one local endpoint is the direct
  mechanism. The literature agrees on the mechanism rather than the tuning:
  intrinsic self-correction without external tool-grounded feedback does not
  reliably improve small-model factual accuracy, and multi-agent verification
  layers frequently lose to a single agent at fixed compute. Allbert's critics
  have no tool-grounded oracle available and the no-regex/no-domain-oracle
  constraint stays, so the layer buys failure modes and latency without an
  information source that could close the gap. The code is unreleased, so
  deletion is migration-free; the evidence and the lesson stay recorded here and
  the implementation stays in git history.
- **Full adaptive fan-out still ships in v1.3.** Manager-planned admission was
  never the failing component — it planned and admitted exactly two children from
  the uncounted FOV-4 prompt on the default 7B, refused dependent sequences, and
  both counted protocols parsed deterministically. Deferring adaptive admission
  to a point release would defer the working half to escape a failure in the
  other half, and would pay the FOV setup cost twice. With the parity bar and the
  critic deletion applied, no observed failure mode remains open.
- Sequence: freeze the single-turn control first so the baseline cannot be
  contaminated by this milestone's edits, then implement the deletion, single-call
  children, Budget v3, and same-endpoint serialization, then focused rejoin,
  configured-provider matrix, agent FOV, attended operator FOV, and only then the
  full SV-0 through SV-9 source pass. No aggregate, package construction, model
  default, or memory floor change is authorized by these decisions. Intake
  candidates pending operator disposition: none.

- The same clean SHA ran `mix allbert.test bench-v13-fanout --mixed-mistral`,

  synthesis to `mistral_small31_24b_challenger`/`mistral-small3.1:24b-instruct-2503-q4_K_M`.
  Identical code, identical prompt, identical fixture; only the manager and
  synthesis heads differ from the default run above.
- **Admission is a model capability limit, not a defect.** The 24B manager
  recorded `manager 2/2`: FOV-4 planned exactly two children with
  `join_role=parent_presentation_only`, the accepted shape. The 7B
  deterministically returns `request_ownership=transform_supplied_content` for
  the same prompt. The two closed enum descriptions it chooses between both
  hinge on the word "list" — `transform_supplied_content` covers requests that
  "transform embedded ... command, or list content", while
  `perform_requested_operations` covers "numbered or listed operations". The 7B
  cannot separate them on this prompt; the 24B can. No code, prompt, fixture, or
  policy was changed to obtain this result.
- **The composer's one-call contract is now attested against a real provider.**
  With admission passing, composition rows were independently evaluated for the
  first time under the new topology. `composer-complementary-architecture` and
  `composer-unrelated-domain-culinary` passed with exactly
  `generation_call_count=1` and `provider_call_count=1`. This is the first
  configured-provider evidence for the M9.b.6 single-call claim; previously only
  owner tests attested it.
- **Five of seven synthesis rows fail layout coverage on the 24B.** All five
  failed at stage `synthesis_layout` with
  `incomplete_fanout_report_composition_selection`: the model's returned
  sections do not partition every completed child queue position exactly once.
  This is model compliance with the frozen layout schema, not an orchestration
  fault, and the composer stores its `deterministic_fallback` report for these
  cases, so the product still delivers a truthful joined report.
- **Recorded evidence defect: failed composition rows null their call counts.**
  `composition_row/5` bails out of its `with` chain before `phase_evidence/1`,
  so `failed_composition/3` writes `generation_call_count=None` and
  `provider_call_count=None` even when the provider was called and returned an
  invalid layout. The five failing rows above each consumed one real provider
  call that the evidence reports as null, so the row under-reports actual
  provider usage on every failure path. M9.b.6 acceptance requires non-null
  per-row attempt counts; this is the composer-side instance of that
  requirement and is not yet met. Not repaired here — it needs operator
  disposition together with the admission decision, since a repair changes
  recorded gate evidence shape.
- Control behavior is unchanged and independent of the manager head: the same
  `2389`-byte answer and `627293e6...` digest were produced in this run, because
  the control resolves the Worker head and never touches manager or synthesis.

### 2026-08-02 — M9.b.6 admission repaired on the default 7B; operator decisions applied

Operator decisions taken after the mixed-head diagnostic: repair the admission
prompt before considering any model-default change; keep the gate's bar at
"model synthesis must succeed"; repair the null per-row call counts.

- **Admission was a prompt defect, not a capability limit — the earlier
  conclusion is corrected.** Probing `qwen2.5:7b` directly on the closed
  `request_ownership` choice showed the model classifies FOV-4 correctly under
  reworded guidance and incorrectly under the shipped wording, with the same
  model and temperature. The 24B masked the defect by being robust to it. The
  root cause was that the guidance discriminated by *form* — both
  `transform_supplied_content` and `perform_requested_operations` described
  "list" content — so no consistent rule was available to follow.
- **The guidance existed in three places and disagreed after a partial edit.**
  The closed-enum schema doc in
  `intent/fanout_manager/req_llm_implementation.ex`, the
  `:supplied_text_ownership` rule, and the `:closed_manager_output` instruction
  in `intent/fanout_manager/policy.ex` each carried their own copy. Editing only
  the schema doc changed nothing, because the rule and instruction still carried
  the form-based framing. All three now discriminate on what the operator wants
  back: a statement ABOUT supplied material versus work the request is only
  satisfied by carrying out. No enum values, criteria, policy checks, admission
  logic, fixtures, or model defaults changed.
- **Default-head result: `manager 2/2`, deterministic.** Two consecutive clean
  runs on `direct_answer_local`/`qwen2.5:7b` recorded FOV-3 still
  `supplied_data` and FOV-4 now `kind=fanout`,
  `policy_outcome=independent_advisory`, `child_count=2`,
  `join_role=parent_presentation_only`. This closes the FOV-4 admission RED on
  the shipped default and removes the need for a model-default decision.
- **Composition improved from `0/7` to `4/7` on the default head**, which is
  better than the 24B's `2/7`. Four rows pass with exactly
  `generation_call_count=1` and `provider_call_count=1`. Of the three
  remaining, two fail `fixture_expectation`/`layout_mismatch` — the model
  returns a schema-valid layout with a different relationship than the frozen
  fixture expects — and one fails `synthesis_layout`, not partitioning every
  child queue position exactly once.
- **Gate bar unchanged by operator decision.** A rejected model synthesis stays
  a failed row even though the composer stores its deterministic fallback and
  the product still delivers a truthful joined report. `bench-v13-fanout`
  therefore remains RED at `4/7` and is not yet green release evidence; the
  remaining gap is model layout compliance, not orchestration.
- **Null per-row call counts repaired for every path the gate can observe.**
  `composition_row/5` now hands a synthesis result to `composed_row/4`, which
  carries real attempt evidence through every later rejection. On the live run,
  rows reporting real counts went from two of seven to six of seven: both
  `fixture_expectation` rows now record `generation_call_count=1` and
  `provider_call_count=1` instead of null. Two owner rows lock the behavior in
  both directions — a row rejected after its provider call reports that call, and
  a row denied at transport before any call still reports none.
- **Residual gap, recorded.** `composer-partial-data-migration` fails inside
  `SynthesisAgent.run` and still reports null counts. The agent observes the
  attempt counts on its error path and discards them, returning a bare
  `{:error, reason}`. Surfacing them requires changing the production error
  contract that `ReportComposer` consumes, which is outside M9.b.6's authorized
  scope; it is carried as a known gap against the non-null acceptance criterion.
- Static and focused state at this SHA: warnings-free compile, formatter, Credo
  strict, Dialyzer `0` errors, docs gate clean, manifest `4,465` rows. Gate,
  manager, fan-out, and runtime-acknowledgement suites green apart from the
  pre-existing `objectives/fanout_test.exs` recovery-wake row and the
  pre-existing `intent/eval/corpus_completeness_test.exs`
  `set_direct_answer_model_profile` row, both of which reproduce unchanged
  without these edits.

### 2026-08-02 — M9.b.6 synthesis specification defects repaired; residual is model judgment

Investigating the `4/7` composition RED under the operator's "model synthesis
must succeed" bar found three specification defects rather than a model-quality
wall. All three are the same class as the admission defect: the model was asked
to make a closed choice with no rule available to follow.

- **The `relationship` enum carried no definition of any value.** The provider
  schema offered `complementary | contrasting | sequential | supporting |
  independent` as bare strings, and the only prompt guidance was "choose
  relationship sections supported by the supplied observations". Nothing said
  what any of the five words meant. Each value now carries its meaning in both
  the schema description and the `relationship_support` rule.
- **The coverage rule named a field that does not exist.** It instructed the
  model to "list every completed queue position once in ascending
  `covered_queue_positions`", while the schema field is
  `ordered_queue_positions`. The model was being directed to populate a field
  it was never offered. Corrected, and the exactly-once and completed-only
  constraints are now stated in the rule as well as the array description.
- **Nothing forbade an empty section or bounded the section count.** Probing the
  default head showed its dominant failure was a correct first section followed
  by a spurious second section, frequently with an empty position list. The
  rules now require every section to carry at least one position, ask for the
  fewest sections that describe the observations, and fix the single-position
  case to `independent`.
- **Both heads improved substantially on identical fixtures.** Default
  `qwen2.5:7b` went from `0/7` to `4/7`; the 24B went from `2/7` to `5/7`. The
  gain on two unrelated models on an unchanged fixture is what distinguishes a
  specification repair from fixture over-specification. The default-head result
  is deterministic: two consecutive clean runs failed the identical three rows,
  and the parity-control digest `627293e6...` was unchanged across every run in
  this milestone.
- **Deliberately stopped short of `7/7`.** The residual failures are
  relationship judgment, not schema compliance. `composer-independent-travel`
  fails on *both* heads: it is the only three-child case and requires splitting
  into `supporting [0,1]` plus `independent [2]`, which neither model chooses.
  Each further prompt edit reshuffled *which* rows passed while leaving the
  total near constant, which is the signature of a model at its ceiling rather
  than a remaining specification gap. Continuing to edit prompt text until the
  fixture is satisfied would overfit the gate instead of improving the product,
  so it was not done.
- **Consequence for release evidence.** Under the operator's retained bar,
  `bench-v13-fanout` stays RED at `4/7` on the default head and is not green
  release evidence. Orchestration is not implicated in any remaining failure:
  the manager admits correctly, each healthy owner makes exactly one provider
  call, and the composer stores its deterministic fallback for every rejected
  synthesis, so the delivered report stays truthful. The open decision is
  whether v1.3 accepts a fallback-backed report as passing, selects a stronger
  synthesis head, or revises the three frozen fixture expectations — all of
  which are operator calls, not implementation work.
- Static and focused state: warnings-free compile, formatter, Credo strict,
  Dialyzer `0` errors, docs gate clean, manifest `4,465` rows. Across the
  objectives, intent, dev-gate, and runtime-acknowledgement suites, `733` tests
  run with the `6` pre-existing failures already shown to reproduce without
  these edits and no new failure.

### 2026-08-02 — M9.b.6 composer specification completed; attempt evidence closed

Reopened after operator direction to fix the deferred items properly rather than
carry them as decisions. Two of the three items marked "operator disposition"
in the entries above were repairable and have been repaired.

- **Attempt evidence is now closed on every path, including the residual.**
  `SynthesisAgent.run/5` created its own attempt counter and discarded it with
  its bare `{:error, reason}`, so a row failing inside synthesis could not
  report the call it consumed. `ProviderAttempt.attach_or_reuse/1` now lets an
  owner supply the counter, and the gate owns one per composition row. Callers
  that supply nothing keep the previous behavior exactly, so neither
  `ReportComposer` consumer changes. On the live default-head run all seven
  rows — passing and failing — report `generation_call_count=1` and
  `provider_call_count=1`, and no row hides a call. This closes the M9.b.6
  non-null attempt-count criterion for the composer, and it independently
  attests the one-call contract across all seven configured-provider rows
  rather than only the passing ones.
- **Anti-forgery strengthened as a side effect.** The gate now records the
  attempts it observed rather than the count a result reports. The forged-phase-
  evidence row previously asserted `nil`; it now asserts the observed `1`,
  so a fabricated self-reported count can neither pass a row nor understate the
  compute a run actually spent.
- **The earlier "model ceiling" reading was wrong and is corrected.** The frozen
  fixture's relationship labels are derivable, and from the parent request
  rather than from the child observations: "…how the independent dating
  observations **support** one bounded chronology claim", "…how supervised
  process recovery and durable state replay **complement** each other",
  "**Contrast** the operating trade-offs", "Combine the related transport
  findings **and retain the separate document reminder as an independent
  finding**". The parent request was already in the prompt; nothing told the
  model it carried the relationship cue. The `relationship_support` rule now
  says so.
- **One earlier repair was itself a defect and was removed.** The "return the
  fewest sections" sentence added to suppress spurious sections was directly
  responsible for `composer-independent-travel` collapsing a two-section
  request into one. Once empty sections were forbidden it was redundant as well
  as wrong, and it is replaced by grouping guidance that admits a subset
  standing apart. The single-position-implies-`independent` cardinality
  constraint is now stated as overriding, matching
  `Report.validate_relationship_cardinality/2`, which enforces it in production.
- **Default-head result: `5/7`, deterministic across two clean runs**, up from
  `0/7` before this milestone's specification work and `4/7` before these
  repairs. The 24B records `5/7` on the same fixture. Manager stays `2/2` and
  the parity-control digest `627293e6...` is unchanged across every run.
- **Residual, and why it stops here.** Both remaining rows are one behavior:
  the default head appends a spurious trailing `independent` section — empty on
  `composer-complementary-architecture`, duplicating position `2` on
  `composer-independent-travel`. Its first section is correct in both. This
  resisted prose (an explicit non-empty rule) and grammar (`minItems`/
  `uniqueItems` on the positions array, which the provider path does not appear
  to enforce). No further specification defect is identifiable: the enum is
  defined, the field names are correct, the cardinality rule is absolute, the
  parent-request cue is stated, and empty sections are forbidden in both prose
  and schema. Continuing would mean editing prompt text until this fixture
  passes, which overfits the gate rather than improving the product, so it was
  not done. The deterministic compiler/renderer cannot be given a regex or
  domain oracle to repair the label, because ADR 0021's no-regex/no-domain-oracle
  constraint is deliberate and stays.
- **Open for operator disposition — genuinely, not deferred work.** Under the
  retained bar `bench-v13-fanout` is RED at `5/7` on the default head. Nothing
  remaining implicates orchestration: admission is correct, every owner makes
  exactly one call, every rejected synthesis stores the deterministic fallback,
  and the delivered report stays truthful. The lever is the configured synthesis
  head, which is a separate closed chain from the Worker head and therefore
  changes no Worker hardware floor.
- Static and focused state: warnings-free compile, formatter, Credo strict,
  Dialyzer `0` errors, docs gate clean, manifest `4,465` rows, `733` tests
  across the objectives, dev-gate, intent, and runtime-acknowledgement suites
  with only the `6` pre-existing failures.

### 2026-08-02 — M9.b.6 composer qualification GREEN on the shipped default

Operator direction: the rules and gates are the operator's own and may change,
provided Allbert stays functional against the vision. Re-examining the `5/7`
RED under that latitude found one product defect and one gate defect, and the
default head now passes.

- **Product defect: a usable synthesis was discarded over a no-op.** A response
  carrying a correct partition plus one stray empty section was rejected whole,
  so the operator received the deterministic fallback instead of a good advisory
  paragraph. A section with no queue positions describes no child, so removing
  it changes nothing the report covers. `ReqLLMImplementation` now drops empty
  sections while normalizing raw provider output. This is not repair of model
  content: any section that claims a position still faces unchanged validation,
  and a response whose sections were all empty still fails closed. The advisory
  paragraph is the model's actual contribution, and losing it to a discardable
  artifact was a real quality loss for real operators, not only a gate number.
- **Gate defect: the fixture pinned taste as if it were truth.** The relationship
  word selects a heading and an intro sentence and nothing else — it does not
  touch child status, ordering, the authoritative appendix, receipts, or
  delivery. Six of the seven parent requests name their relationship outright
  ("Contrast the operating trade-offs", "how they complement each other",
  "support one bounded chronology claim", "as an independent finding"), and
  `composer-partial-data-migration` is forced by cardinality. Exactly one place
  was over-specified: `composer-independent-travel`'s first section, whose
  request says only "Combine the related transport findings" — naming the
  grouping but not the relation. The fixture chose `supporting`; `complementary`
  is equally defensible for two schedule facts that jointly establish a
  connection.
- **The gate now asserts what is determined and stops asserting taste.** Each
  expected section carries `relationship_source`. The partition is always exact,
  because it is the report's structural claim about which child each section
  covers. The relationship is pinned exactly where the operator's request names
  it, which tests instruction-following; where the request does not name it, any
  label production's cardinality rule already admits is accepted. Owner rows
  prove all three edges: a request-named label must be honored, an unnamed label
  may differ, and an unnamed label still cannot move a position. Fixture digest
  is now `0e18ee567d76021a8816a0345dba80f52bf463d72c9699d1cfac308dd64aa0d1`.
- **One more specification defect, found by the model's own behavior.** With
  empty sections impossible, the default head began emitting the correct section
  followed by a per-child `independent` section for each position it had already
  placed. The rules said "never repeat a position" but never said sections
  *partition*. They now state that every completed child appears in exactly one
  section and that children in the same relationship belong together rather than
  each receiving a section of its own.
- **Result: `bench-v13-fanout --profile direct_answer_local` is GREEN.** Five
  consecutive clean runs on `local_ollama/qwen2.5:7b` recorded `status=passed`,
  manager `2/2`, composition `7/7`, every row at exactly one generation and one
  provider call, and the parity-control digest `627293e6...` unchanged. The
  progression on the shipped default across this milestone is `0/7` → `4/7` →
  `5/7` → `7/7`, with no fixture expectation weakened except the single
  unnamed-relationship section above.
- **Recorded caveat: the `--mixed-mistral` comparison sits at `4/7`.** It is a
  frozen diagnostic mode, not a shipping configuration, and the 24B is not the
  qualification target. Probed directly through Ollama's native structured-output
  path on the identical prompts it returns the exactly-correct sections for the
  rows the gate fails, so the discrepancy appears to be the ReqLLM
  OpenAI-compatible structured-output path rather than model semantics. That is
  an integration question outside M9.b.6 and is carried as an intake candidate,
  not a blocker; the shipped default does not use it.
- Static and focused state: warnings-free compile, formatter, Credo strict,
  Dialyzer `0` errors, docs gate clean, manifest reconciled, `736` tests across
  the objectives, dev-gate, intent, and runtime-acknowledgement suites with only
  the `6` pre-existing failures that reproduce without these edits.

### 2026-08-02 — agent FOV rehearsal: orchestration GREEN, content parity RED

Ran at exact clean pushed `62523b89` on a disposable Home against the configured
`local_ollama/qwen2.5:7b`. Two coverage limits are recorded up front: the agent
shell cannot drive the interactive alternate-screen TUI, so FOV-2 and the TUI
presentation criteria of FOV-4 were **not** exercised, and the FOV-4 turn was
submitted through `mix allbert.ask` rather than the attached TUI. Everything
below therefore covers Runtime, Objectives, Worker, composer, and the durable
record, not the TUI surface. The operator's attended run still owns those rows.

- **FOV-0 PASS.** Clean pushed SHA verified against `origin/main`, empty working
  tree, warnings-free `MIX_ENV=dev` compile, private Home, migration, daemon
  healthy on `4137`.
- **FOV-1 PASS** on the reconciled step. The pre-reconciliation version would
  have failed here: it read `model_preferences.tasks.fanout_review` and grepped
  for its Model Doctor row, and that key no longer exists.
- **FOV-4 orchestration PASS.** Allbert announced exactly two meaningful
  children and returned the prompt while they ran, with diagnostics
  `result=fanout`, `outcome=planned`, `attempts=1`, `work_unit_count=2`,
  `join_role=parent_presentation_only`,
  `policy_outcome=independent_advisory`, `fanout_admission=admitted`. Pre-egress
  disclosure named only the manager and synthesis routes, confirming the
  `fanout_review` route is gone from the operator-visible path.
- **FOV-7R PASS, and it independently verifies the reconciliation.** Every
  reconciled expectation matched live durable evidence exactly: topology
  `1|2|2|0|completed|success|acknowledged|ready|model`; budget
  `3|64|4|32768|11264|300000`, which is Budget v3's
  `manager + child_count + composer` geometry; receipts `2|2|2|2|2|2|2|2` with
  quality-receipt v3, rule-catalog v2, and `generation_call_count=1` and
  `provider_call_count=1` on both children; selection
  `1|model|2|accepted|3|1|1|complementary|[0,1]|[0,1]|1`. The report carried the
  exact status totals, `Completed synthesis:`, the relationship line, the
  labelled advisory paragraph, and the ordered authoritative appendix with both
  receipt digests, at `3,225` bytes against the `32,768` bound.
- **FOV-4 content parity RED — this is the finding.** Both children's durable
  results are meta-descriptions of an analysis rather than the analysis: child 0
  is `169` characters ("A detailed analysis of OTP supervision tree behavior
  under failure scenarios, highlighting the impact of different restart policies
  such as one_for_one and rest_for_one.") and child 1 is `420`. These are the
  full durable results, not a truncated projection. Against the frozen `2,389`
  byte control on the identical prompt, which did state mechanisms
  substantively, this fails the parity rubric on coverage of the asked
  sub-question and on completeness within bounds: neither child ever states the
  `one_for_one`/`rest_for_one` difference the request asked for, and the output
  sits three orders of magnitude below the available bound. Decomposition made
  the answer materially worse than the same head answering in one turn, which is
  exactly what this bar exists to detect. Child 1 additionally absorbs child 0's
  topic into its own text, so the two are not cleanly separated.
- **Root-cause lead, not yet closed.** Each child's result closely mirrors its
  own manager-compiled `acceptance_criteria.summary`, which reads "A detailed
  analysis of ... highlighting the impact of different restart policies" — the
  child appears to restate its expected result instead of performing the
  objective. A trial edit to `Grounding.compiled_task_input/1`, which builds
  `direct_answer_text` and is confirmed on the live path, produced
  byte-identical child results across a second full run on a fresh Home. That
  rules out prompt wording as the sole cause and means the field is either not
  reaching the model as assumed or is being overwritten downstream. The trial
  edit was reverted rather than committed unproven; closing this needs a focused
  trace from `compiled_task_input/1` to the physical request and back to
  `objective_steps.result_summary`.
- **Disposition.** Orchestration, durable evidence, budget, receipt, and
  selection contracts are green and the reconciled runbook is verified correct
  against live data. Operator FOV should not begin until the parity RED is
  root-caused and repaired, because FOV-4's content criteria would fail for the
  operator exactly as they failed here. No defaults, catalog entries, or gates
  were changed in response to this RED.


### 2026-08-02 — parity RED root-caused and fixed; composer fallback now open

- **Root cause located by two measurements.** The receipt's
  `final_answer_sha256` matched the 169-character meta-description exactly, so
  DirectAnswer returned that text and nothing downstream discarded a longer
  answer. Sending the model the exact stored step params returned `4,481`
  characters of real analysis, so the head was capable and was not receiving
  those params. `JidoAdapter` overrides `:text` with
  `QualityPolicy.draft_prompt/1` at execution time, so the durable step spec is
  not the model input — which is why an earlier edit to
  `Grounding.compiled_task_input/1` changed the stored params and left the
  result byte-identical.
- **The defect was a collision of two correct-in-isolation rules.**
  `draft_prompt/1` asked the model to "complete the child task represented by
  this verified contract" and handed it a JSON contract whose `expected_result`
  already reads as a finished one-line summary, while the registered
  DirectAnswer route carries the conversational rule "keep the answer useful,
  factual, direct, and brief". Brevity is right for a chat turn and wrong for a
  delegated analysis that becomes a report section, and a small head satisfies
  both at once by returning the summary.
- **Fixed and verified.** `draft_prompt/1` now states that the answer is the
  deliverable and must carry the actual findings, distinctions, and mechanisms
  at whatever length that needs; that conversational brevity does not apply; and
  that `expected_result` is a checklist to satisfy rather than text to return,
  with restating it named as a failed result. On a fresh disposable Home the
  child results went from `169`/`420` characters to `2,000`/`2,000` — the
  `ObservationSummary` durable cap, so the underlying answers are longer — and
  the text is real analysis with explicit `one_for_one`/`rest_for_one`
  treatment. `draft_prompt/1` is derived from the contract but is not
  digest-bound, so no receipt, rule-catalog version, or task-contract digest
  changed. `bench-v13-fanout` stays green at manager `2/2`, composition `7/7`.
- **New open item: the composer falls back on realistic children.** With
  substantive observations the live composer returns `invalid_model_output` and
  the parent stores `deterministic_fallback`, so the joined report is truthful
  but not model-synthesised. Reproduced deterministically outside the product by
  replaying the exact live observations through `ReqLLMImplementation.prompt/1`:
  the head returns `[complementary [0,1], supporting [0]]`, repeating position
  `0` in a trailing section. Ruled out along the way: input size is not the cause
  (an `8.3` KB prompt succeeds at both a `1,024` and `2,048` token cap), and the
  transport is not the cause (Ollama's native `format` path and its
  OpenAI-compatible `json_schema` path both return valid output at that size).
- **Why the gate did not catch it.** The frozen composition fixtures carry child
  observations of roughly `80`–`115` characters, while real children now emit
  `2,000`. The composer qualification is therefore passing on inputs about
  twenty times smaller than live output, and the trailing-duplicate tic that the
  M9.b.6 partition rule suppressed at fixture size returns at realistic size.
  Making the fixture representative is the honest repair and will very likely
  turn `bench-v13-fanout` RED until the composer handles it.
- **Diagnosed to a deterministic model failure; three repairs tried and
  reverted.** Replaying the exact live snapshot through
  `ReqLLMImplementation.compose/3` and `Report.prepare_synthesis/2` reproduces
  the failure identically on every run. The head returns
  `[sequential [0], supporting [1]]`: it covers both children but gives each its
  own section under a *relational* label, and production correctly rejects a
  single-position relational section, since `complementary`/`contrasting`/
  `sequential`/`supporting` all assert a relationship between at least two
  observations. The rules already tell the model to group children that stand in
  the same relationship rather than giving each a section of its own; it does not
  comply on this input. Tried and reverted, each because it failed an honest
  check rather than because it was unfinished: naming the exact required
  positions in the instruction (did not fix the live case and regressed the gate
  from `7/7` to `3/7`); completing the partition by placing an omitted child as
  its own `independent` section (leaves the model's own single-position
  relational section invalid, so the layout still fails); and bounding the
  synthesis observation input to `768` bytes (the head still returns one
  relational section per child).
- **A measurement error is recorded with them.** The `768`-byte reading that
  first looked promising, and the check that appeared to confirm the partition
  repair, both summed queue positions *across* sections and never asked whether
  the layout would validate. Aggregate coverage is not validity. The conclusions
  above rest on `Report.prepare_synthesis/2` returning `{:ok, _}` or an error,
  which is the same predicate production uses.
- **Assessment.** This is a capability limit of the configured `qwen2.5:7b` on
  realistic analysis text, not an orchestration defect, and Allbert's response
  is the designed one: the composer stores the deterministic complete-child
  report, which is truthful and complete. The levers that remain are operator
  decisions rather than implementation work — select a stronger synthesis head
  (a separate closed chain from the Worker head, so no Worker hardware floor
  changes), or revisit whether
  `fanout_report_relationship_section_required` should keep forcing a relational
  grouping when the model genuinely finds none, since two honestly independent
  sections are arguably a truthful layout that the rule currently rejects.
- **Consequence for the operator FOV.** FOV-4's structural, budget, receipt, and
  parity rows are expected to pass. Its `report_source` row will show
  `deterministic_fallback` rather than `model` until this is closed. That is
  degraded-but-truthful product behavior, not a correctness failure, and the
  runbook says so explicitly rather than letting the operator hit an
  unexplained mismatch.

### 2026-08-02 — M9.b.7 plan: deterministic layout, model-judged relationship

Operator decision: adopt the simplified synthesis contract. The composer stops
asking the model to restate a partition Allbert already owns and asks it only
for the judgment it can make.

**Why.** JSON Schema and constrained decoding enforce syntax and structure but
not semantics, so the partition, cardinality, and relational-section invariants
can never be grammar-enforced; the model satisfies them by instruction or not at
all. Published work also finds reasoning degrades under format restriction in
proportion to its rigidity, and that adapting schemas to small models beats
adapting the models. Measured on the live snapshot through production's own
validity predicate, the shipped `qwen2.5:7b` produced a valid layout `0/2` under
the current array-of-sections schema and `5/5` under a single-relationship
schema; `mistral-small3.1:24b` and `gemma4:31b` pass both, while `qwen3.5:27b`
and `gpt-oss:20b` return empty content and are not usable heads here.

**Contract.** The provider returns exactly `relationship` (the existing closed
enum) and `advisory_synthesis`. Allbert deterministically builds one section
covering every completed queue position in ascending order, and forces
`independent` when exactly one child completed, which is what
`validate_relationship_cardinality/2` already requires. The stored selection
shape, layout v2, provenance, digests, `Report` validation, delivery, and the
deterministic fallback are all unchanged, so no replay path moves and
`synthesis_contract_version` does not bump; the request change is already
carried by the configuration digest through `response_schema_sha256`.

**Rejected during design, with evidence.** An optional outlier field was
prototyped twice. Named `standalone_positions` the shipped head misread it as
"positions in this relationship" and marked whole sets standalone; renamed
`unrelated_positions` with an explicit rarity hint it went unused, and on
`composer-independent-travel` neither the 7B nor the 24B identified the outlier
(`complementary []` and `sequential []`). A field neither head uses correctly and
one head actively misuses is removed rather than shipped.

**Accepted capability reduction, stated plainly.** Model-chosen sub-grouping is
gone. `composer-independent-travel` asks to "retain the separate document
reminder as an independent finding" and will now render one section over all
three children instead of `supporting [0,1]` plus `independent [2]`. Its frozen
expectation changes to the single-section layout and the fixture digest rotates.
Every child still appears exactly once, in order, in the authoritative appendix,
and the advisory paragraph may still note the separation; what is lost is the
structural split, in exchange for the composer working at all on the shipped
default.

**Acceptance.** Composer owner tests prove the provider schema shape, the
deterministic section construction, and the single-completed-child cardinality
forcing. `bench-v13-fanout --profile direct_answer_local` passes on the default
head. A live disposable-Home FOV-4 stores `report_source=model` rather than
`deterministic_fallback`. Formatter, Credo strict, Dialyzer, docs gate, and the
manifest stay clean, with no new failure beyond the recorded pre-existing rows.

  `Report.validate_relationship_cardinality/2`. The stored selection shape,

### 2026-08-02 — attended FOV-7R found two runbook assertion defects, not product defects

The operator's attended run passed FOV-0, FOV-1, FOV-2, and FOV-4, then failed
FOV-7R on two rows. Both were defects in the runbook's own SQL, proven against
the operator's live Home rather than argued.

- **Synthesis binding extracted the wrong bytes.** The check located the
  advisory paragraph with the prefix `Model-authored advisory synthesis:` plus
  one newline, but the renderer emits a blank line and a `> ` blockquote marker
  before the paragraph, so the check hashed `"\n> " <> paragraph`. The stored
  `synthesis_sha256` is correct: hashing the paragraph with those three bytes
  stripped reproduces `d7d420ab…` exactly, matching the event. The prefix now
  includes the blank line and the blockquote marker, and the corrected query
  returns the event digest byte for byte.
- **Report-state asserted verbatim inclusion of the full observation.** Columns
  six, seven, and nine required each child's complete durable
  `last_observation_summary` to appear inside `report_body`. The appendix
  renders a bounded, newline-escaped excerpt, so a real multi-line observation
  can never appear verbatim; even a 500-character prefix does not match. The
  assertion only ever held because pre-M9.b.7 children returned single-line
  answers of 169 and 420 characters. It now compares each observation's first
  line, which is present, unique, and correctly ordered at report offsets 2,622
  and 5,077 in the operator's Home.
- **Verified end to end.** Re-running the corrected block against the operator's
  unchanged Home returns `PASS FOV-7R` with
  `durable-report-state` `ready|model|delivered|2|2|2|2|1|1` and
  `durable-synthesis-binding` `1`, alongside the already-passing
  `3|64|4|32768|11264|300000|1`, `2|2|2|2|2|2|2|2`, and
  `1|model|2|accepted|3|1|1|complementary|[0,1]|[0,1]|1`.
- **Recorded for the operator's convention on runbooks.** FOV-7R is a
  371-line paste-executable block. That length is itself a hazard for an
  attended step and is a candidate for extraction into a checked-in script the
  runbook invokes, once validation closes.

### 2026-08-02 — FOV-8 manifest defect; attended focused qualification closed

- **Third runbook defect, same class as the first two.** FOV-8's SHA-256
  evidence manifest still listed `fanout-review.txt`. FOV-1 stopped producing
  that file when the `fanout_review` chain was removed, so FOV-8 could never

- **The operator's paste was corrupted by the terminal, not by the script.** The
  153-line block wrapped and interleaved in the shell buffer and bash reported a

  is itself the clean-shutdown evidence FOV-8 asserts. Nothing was lost and the

- **Attended focused qualification is closed.** FOV-0, FOV-1, FOV-2, FOV-4,
  FOV-7R, and FOV-8 all pass on the operator's machine at exact clean pushed
  `2775521e`, with the durable evidence recorded in the entries above. Full
  SV-0 through SV-9 may start from a fresh Home.
- **Runbook extraction is now a required follow-up, not a nice-to-have.** Three
  defects in one attended run — the `fanout_review` read in FOV-1, two assertion
  errors in FOV-7R, and this manifest entry — all lived in blocks of 150 to 371
  lines that no test executes. Extracting them into checked-in scripts the
  runbook invokes, with the expected values as data, would have caught all three

1. `qwen2.5:7b` states that `rest_for_one` restarts every child. That describes
