### 2026-08-02 — M9.b.8: DirectAnswer head factual qualification (DISPOSITIONED — disclosed here, bar deferred to v1.3.1)

The attended FOV surfaced a defect no orchestration work can reach: the shipped
head states OTP restart semantics incorrectly, in the joined report an operator
reads. Probed directly on the identical question, only one locally available
head is correct.

| head | `rest_for_one` answer | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| `qwen2.5:7b` (shipped default) | "only the specific child process that has crashed is restarted" | wrong — that is `one_for_one` |
| `llama3.1:8b` | children started after are restarted, but the crashed process is not | wrong |
| `mistral-small3.1:24b` | "only the crashed child process is restarted" | wrong |
| `gemma4:31b` | "that specific process and any other children started after it" | correct |
| `qwen3.5:27b` | no content returned | unusable through this path |

- **Scope.** This is DirectAnswer task-head qualification, which M9.b.6 named as
  the owner of absolute answer correctness when it moved fan-out to a parity
  bar. Fan-out's bar is unaffected and passed: the frozen single-turn control
  contains the same class of error, so decomposition did not degrade the answer.
- **Not repaired here, deliberately.** Changing `model_preferences.tasks.direct_answer`
  is a model-default change, which the recorded 2026-08-02 decisions explicitly
  do not authorize. `gemma4:31b` is a candidate on this evidence, but one probe
  on one topic is not a qualification bar, and raising the DirectAnswer head
  moves the Worker hardware floor for every operator — unlike the synthesis
  chain, which is separate.
- **Needed before v1.3 ships.** A decision on whether v1.3 ships with a head
  known to be wrong on its own flagship demonstration prompt, and if a bar is
  wanted, a small frozen factual corpus with a stated pass mark rather than a
  single ad-hoc probe.
- **Second data point, 2026-08-02 attended SV.** SV-6A.1a.ii failed when the
  same head turned an acknowledgment into a commitment. That is
  instruction-following under load rather than factual knowledge, and it was
  not reproducible in thirty-six trials, so the bar this milestone defines
  should cover reliability as well as accuracy: a corpus that only tests facts
  would have passed the head on this row.

#### Operator disposition, 2026-08-02 — v1.3 discloses, v1.3.1 measures

The operator decided v1.3 ships the default head unchanged and states its
limits, rather than raising a model default on one probe or holding the release
for a bar that does not exist yet. What ships in v1.3:

- **Both failure modes disclosed** in `docs/operator/model-recommendations.md` —
  the `rest_for_one` factual error with the cross-head table showing that
  parameter count predicts nothing, and the acknowledgment-to-commitment error
  with its observed rate (once in thirty-seven), plus the standing instruction
  never to read a future-tense answer as evidence that anything was scheduled.
- **The opt-in seam documented**: `allbert admin settings set
  intent.direct_answer_model_profile <profile>`, with the disk/memory trade-off
  stated (4.7 GB default versus roughly 20 GB for a 31B head, which will not run
  on a 16 GB machine) and the reason Allbert does not raise the default itself —
  the hardware floor is the operator's constraint, not Allbert's preference.
- **An explicit admission that no qualification bar exists today**, so an
  operator selecting a head knows the burden is theirs.

The bar itself opens as **v1.3.1** — `docs/plans/v1.3.1-plan.md` +
request-flow + **ADR 0097** — rather than expanding v1.3 or being folded into
v1.4, which is left untouched. Its scope is fixed by this milestone's two data
points: a frozen digest-bound corpus covering **both** failure families, since a
facts-only corpus would have passed the head on the acknowledgment row; closed
deterministic scoring with no model judging another model (M9.b.6); N trials per
row against a minimum frozen before any head is measured, since single-shot
scoring would have called this head compliant on thirty-six of thirty-seven
observations; requests through the production DirectAnswer path; and no change
to the shipped default, which stays a separate operator decision that consumes
the evidence rather than one this milestone makes.

M9.b.8 is closed for v1.3 purposes. It is not a ship blocker.

FOV-0 through FOV-8 completed attended and all passed, so the scripts' remaining

  (`artifacts_browser_seed.exs`, `voice_live_smoke.exs`, `vision_live_smoke.exs`,

**The durable lesson does not archive with them.** Four defect classes reached
the operator's terminal because they lived in runbook prose no test executes.
The v1.3.1 request flow states that its HQ commands are written at build time
against real code rather than guessed, and any future release that ships an
attended runbook inherits the same obligation. A general runbook-executability
guard is worth building once the shape stabilizes across two releases; it is not
v1.3 work.

### M9.b.9 — Truthful advisory vocabulary (ship blocker)

`[code]` `[docs]` `[gate]`

M9.b.6 deleted every reviewer. The vocabulary those reviewers justified stayed,
so Allbert now asserts a review it does not perform, in the report an operator
reads.

**The defect.** Both remaining verdicts are hardcoded literals with no evaluator
behind them: `Runs.Worker.Commands.Execute` writes `"verdict" => "accepted"`, and
`ReportComposer.Commands.Synthesize` writes `"verdict" => "accepted"` plus every
catalog rule marked `"satisfied"`. The rendered report then tells the operator
`Generated advisory observation`, `Result authority: reviewed_advisory`, and
`receipt verifies advisory quality, not effect evidence`. All three are false.
The receipt binds objective, step, task contract, generator configuration, call
counts, and the answer digest — provenance, not quality.

**Why this is a ship blocker and the model's factual limits are not.** A local
model being wrong is expected, bounded, and now disclosed in
`docs/operator/model-recommendations.md`. Allbert's deterministic layer
overclaiming is different in kind: the entire authority model rests on Allbert's
own statements being exactly true while model output is advisory. An operator who
learns that `reviewed` meant nothing has no reason to trust `completed`,
`delivered`, or an effect receipt either. This was introduced by M9.b.6 and not
revisited when its reviewers were removed.

- Rename the durable authority `reviewed_advisory` to `generated_advisory`
  across the frozen child envelope, `@result_authorities`, and every validator.
  `legacy_unreviewed_advisory` keeps its meaning and its replay path.
- Replace the appendix label `Generated advisory observation (not effect
  evidence)` with `Generated advisory observation (not effect evidence)`, and
  the authority line's `receipt verifies advisory quality, not effect evidence`
  with a statement of what the receipt actually binds.
- Replace the worker receipt's `verdict` with an `outcome` that names what
  happened rather than judging it, and rename `rule_catalog_version` to
  `instructed_rule_catalog_version` so it cannot be read as rules that were
  checked. Receipt version `3` becomes `4`; version `3` and earlier stay
  replay-only.
- Remove the composer's self-certifying `rule_results`, in which the same code
  path that produced the candidate marked every rule satisfied. Keep whatever
  the selection contract genuinely needs for validation, and name it for what it
  is.
- No behavior, call count, budget, delivery path, or fallback changes. This
  milestone changes only what Allbert claims about its own output.

Acceptance: no shipped string or durable field asserts review, verification, or
quality where none is performed; owner tests pin the new vocabulary and the
absent old one; `bench-v13-fanout` stays green on the shipped default; a live
disposable-Home report shows the corrected wording; FOV-7R and the frozen fixture
are reconciled to the renamed authority.

### 2026-08-02 — M9.b.9 implemented and verified: Allbert no longer claims a review it does not perform

- **Durable authority renamed.** `reviewed_advisory` is now `generated_advisory`
  across the frozen child envelope, `@result_authorities`, every validator, the
  frozen fixture, and the FOV runbook. `legacy_unreviewed_advisory` keeps its
  meaning and replay path untouched.
- **Operator-facing wording corrected.** The appendix label is now `Generated
  advisory observation (not verified, not effect evidence)`, and the authority
  line reads `receipt binds this answer to its task, generator configuration,
  and call count; nothing verifies the answer's content.` A live disposable-Home
  report at `7,620` bytes carries both and contains zero occurrences of
  `Reviewed advisory`, `verifies advisory quality`, or `reviewed_advisory`.
- **Receipt v4.** `verdict: "accepted"` becomes `outcome: "generated"`, which
  states what happened instead of judging it, and `rule_catalog_version` becomes
  `instructed_rule_catalog_version` so it cannot be read as rules that were
  checked. The digest domain moves to `v4`; versions `3` and earlier stay
  replay-only, and the legacy v1 shape keeps its original `verdict` key.
- **Composer self-certification deleted.** `locally_reviewed/2` became
  `locally_validated/2`: it records the deterministic coverage check it actually
  performs as `validation: %{outcome: "passed", covered_queue_positions: ...}`.
  The former `rule_results` block, in which the same code path that produced the
  candidate marked every catalog rule `satisfied`, is gone rather than renamed.
  Selection provenance follows: `review_verdict` is `validation_outcome`
  (`"passed"`), and `reviewed_queue_positions` is `covered_queue_positions`.
- **Two defects caught during the change.** A bulk rename silently reordered two
  `provenance_keys/1` literals that must be sorted, which surfaced as
  `selected_body_invalid` on every composition row; and it rewrote the frozen
  legacy v1 receipt's `verdict` key, which would have broken historical replay.
  Both were repaired and the second is now pinned by the legacy shape assertion.
- **State.** `bench-v13-fanout --profile direct_answer_local` green at manager
  `2/2`, composition `7/7`; live report `report_source=model`; formatter, Credo
  strict, Dialyzer `0`, docs gate, manifest reconciled; `451` tests across the
  objectives, dev-gate, models, and runtime-acknowledgement suites with only the
  `5` pre-existing failures. Fixture digest is now
  `0e18ee567d76021a8816a0345dba80f52bf463d72c9699d1cfac308dd64aa0d1`.
- **No behavior changed.** Call counts, budgets, delivery, fallback, and
  admission are byte-identical in effect. Only what Allbert claims about its own
  output changed.

The operator ran SV-0 through SV-5B on their own machine at exact clean pushed
source and reported each result directly. SV-0 passed under the strengthened
`--force` rebuild: `1,135` plus `57` files compiled warnings-free, which is the
first evidence that the two Linux warnings are genuinely gone rather than hidden
by a warm `_build`.

- `PASS SV-0` through `PASS SV-5`: clean pushed source, migrated disposable Home,
  daemon `38232` healthy, both model doctors green, TUI attached with the route
  disclosure rendered, and terminal 2 bound to the same daemon and Home.
- `PASS SV-5B`, the step added today to close the coverage gap. An ordinary
  question answered in one turn with no fan-out; supplied YAML summarized as
  data with no children and no claim that either listed step ran; and the
  counted protocol splitting into exactly two children with one joined report,
  correct status totals, and one `Selected relationship:` line.
- **M9.b.9 confirmed in an attended session.** The report carried `Generated
  advisory observation (not verified, not effect evidence)` and `Result
  authority: generated_advisory; ... receipt binds this answer to its task,
  generator configuration, and call count; nothing verifies the answer's

  own output truthfully.

  SV-5 predated SV-5B and would have skipped the only SV step exercising
  fan-out; and SV-4 introduced its expected output with wording that reads as an
  instruction to type it, which the operator reasonably did, producing one
  provider call and a stray conversation turn. Both repaired. The stray turn is
  harmless to the remaining rows, which assert on content rather than counts.
- **A false finding is recorded against the assistant, not the product.** That
  stray turn was first reported as a TUI defect feeding the disclosure back as
  operator input, with database evidence called decisive. The operator supplied
  the actual cause. The evidence was real; the conclusion was drawn before the
  simplest explanation was ruled out.

### 2026-08-02 — SV-6A.1a.ii RED: acknowledgment became a commitment; not reproducible

The attended run failed SV-6A.1a.ii. Asked to acknowledge a stated preference,
the shipped head answered "Status summaries for Project Juniper validation
**will be provided** via the 'juniper-v13-primary' marker starting June 1, 2026,
at 09:00 on Fridays." All four required values are present, but the row
explicitly fails future-tense language claiming Allbert will provide, schedule,
store, send, or perform something. Allbert has scheduled nothing. It also
misreads the validation marker as a delivery channel.

- **The rule exists and is correct.** `DirectAnswer.Policy` carries
  `:acknowledgments_are_not_commitments` with criteria
  `no_future_commitment`: "Use a direct present-tense restatement for an
  acknowledgment. Never promise future work, create a schedule, or imply that an
  effect occurred." Nothing enforces it, which is exactly what M9.b.9 stopped
  Allbert from pretending about.
- **Not reproducible in 36 trials across four conditions.** Three isolated,
  three with a short preceding exchange, three replaying the operator's exact
  twelve-message thread, twelve across varied seeds, and fifteen through the
  real `ReqLLMAnswerer` production path. Every one produced a correct
  present-tense restatement, typically "For Project Juniper validation, you
  prefer status summaries on Friday at 09:00, effective from 2026-06-01, with
  the validation marker juniper-v13-primary."
- **Transport is ruled out.** The composer investigation had shown ReqLLM's
  OpenAI-compatible path behaving differently from Ollama's native one, so that
  was the first suspect. Fifteen runs through `ReqLLMAnswerer` itself reproduce
  nothing.
- **Corrected assumption worth recording.** DirectAnswer's prompt carries no
  conversation history at all — only the instruction, the rule catalog, active
  memory, and the operator input. The history replays were therefore more
  generous than production, and the isolated probe is the faithful one.
- **Most consistent explanation, not a proof.** Temperature `0` does not make
  batched inference deterministic: a serving engine rebatches continuously,
  different batch sizes retile the GPU reduction, and floating-point addition is
  not associative, so logits drift in their last bits and can flip a token.
  Published work sampling one model a thousand times at temperature `0` observed
  eighty distinct outputs. The failing call landed moments after a fan-out had
  put several requests through the same endpoint; every reproduction attempt ran
  against an idle one.
- **Decision: change nothing.** A failure at a base rate under one in
  thirty-six cannot be shown fixed by a reworded rule without hundreds of
  controlled trials, and the batching variable is not controlled across them.
  Rejected for the same reason: a deterministic future-tense check would be a
  regex domain oracle that ADR 0021 forbids and would reinstate the critic
  pattern M9.b.6 deleted.
- **This is the second DirectAnswer content failure in one attended session**,
  after the `rest_for_one` factual error, and it feeds M9.b.8 rather than
  opening a milestone. One probe on one topic was thin evidence for a
  head-qualification bar; two independent live failures on the flagship path is
  a rate an operator would notice. M9.b.8 should now weigh instruction-following
  reliability under load alongside factual accuracy.
- **Consequence for validation.** SV-0 through SV-5B passed and SV-9 closed the
  session cleanly, so the run is complete and honest but not a full SV pass.
  A full SV-0 through SV-9 needs a fresh Home once M9.b.8 is dispositioned,
  because the head decision could change the very behavior this row tests.

### M9.b.10 — Memory projection bootstrap (ship blocker)

`[code]` `[test]` `[docs]`

Found by attended SV on 2026-08-03 at daemon 39068, on a fresh Home at
`1cacb8f67`. **On a fresh Home nothing ever builds the first Memory projection
generation, so a kept claim can never be retrieved.** Memory is v1.3's headline
capability; an operator who keeps a claim and asks Allbert to recall it gets a
hard error.

**Reproduction.** SV-6B.5 kept the consolidated Juniper proposal. The canonical
write is entirely correct — `status=kept`,
`result.append_outcome="appended"`, and the claim stream written to
`memory/notes/<claim_id>.md` carrying its HMAC integrity tag and full
`source_evidence` back to `msg_873dfbca` in `thr_cfee0ad7`. But the same result
records
`projection: {"outcome":"repair_pending","reason":":memory_projection_not_ready"}`,
`projections/memory/` holds only `control.json` with `state=not_ready` and
`current_generation_id=null`, and
`mix allbert admin memory retrieve` fails with
`** (Mix) Memory command failed: :memory_projection_not_ready`.

**Root cause — a bootstrap gap, not a broken write.** ADR 0089 states that "an
incremental accepted/reviewed mutation advances a projection revision; only a
full rebuild or schema/configuration change promotes a new generation." That
presumes a generation already exists, and the ADR never says who creates the
first one. Nothing does:

| path | creates the first generation? |
|---|---|
| `Projection.init/1` | only when pending Forget tombstones exist (`maybe_kick_forget_recovery/1`); a fresh Home has none |
| keep (`ProposalReview.refresh_projection/1`) | no — `refresh_claim/1` requires `ready?: true`, and on failure the code records the string `"repair_pending"` without ever calling `Projection.queue_repair/2` |
| retrieve | no — `Retrieval`'s `queue_repair` fires only when returned results contain invalid entries, which presupposes a working projection |
| first run / onboarding / web | nothing found |

The only creator is `Managed.kick("memory-index-rebuild", "local")`, reachable
solely from `projection.ex:210/216`, and that job's schedule is
`{"kind":"manual"}`.

**Why the suite missed it.** `proposal_review_test.exs` calls
`Projection.rebuild(projection)` explicitly before every retrieve (lines 109,
122, 143). Every test performs by hand the one step production never performs,
so the only path an operator actually takes — keep, then retrieve — has never
been exercised.

**`repair_pending` that never repairs is the M9.b.9 defect class again.** A
deterministic layer naming an outcome after a recovery it does not schedule is
the same overclaim M9.b.9 removed from the fan-out report, in a different
subsystem.

#### M9.b.10.a — build the first generation at startup (do this first)

`[code]`

When `Projection.init/1` finds `state: "not_ready"` with no serving generation
and this node holds the writer lock, bootstrap a first generation rather than
serving errors forever. Operator decision 2026-08-03: close the class at boot
rather than only on the keep trigger.

- Must not block daemon readiness. `/health` and attach must come up on the
  existing path while the build proceeds; SV-2's 90-second bound stands.
- Must be bounded and honest on large claim sets — reuse the existing
  `max_entries` cap and typed partial/degraded progress rather than inventing a
  second path, and never promote an incomplete generation.
- Must be idempotent and safe against a concurrent manual rebuild.
- Must stay a no-op on a node without the writer lock.

Acceptance: on a fresh Home with the daemon started and no manual job run,
`projections/memory/current.sqlite3` exists and control reports `state: "ready"`;
daemon health is unaffected; a Home with an existing ready generation is
untouched.

#### M9.b.10.b — a keep queues its own repair

`[code]`

`ProposalReview.refresh_projection/1` calls `Projection.queue_repair/2` when
`refresh_claim/1` fails, so `repair_pending` names a repair that is actually
scheduled. This remains necessary after M9.b.10.a: a projection can degrade
after boot, and the keep path should heal it rather than record a word.

Acceptance: a keep against a not-ready projection queues the repair and the
recorded outcome remains truthful about what was scheduled.

#### M9.b.10.c — cover the path the suite masks

`[test]`

- Keep → retrieve on a fresh Home with **no** manual `Projection.rebuild` call,
  proving the claim is retrievable through the operator's actual path.
- Daemon boot on a fresh Home promotes a first generation without a job run.
- Existing tests that call `Projection.rebuild/1` explicitly stay, but at least
  one row must prove the unassisted path, or this gap reopens silently.

Acceptance: the new rows fail against the current code and pass after
M9.b.10.a/b.

#### M9.b.10.d — runbook corrections this exposed

`[docs]`

- SV-6B.5 binds `V13_MEMORY_PATH` from `mix allbert admin memory list --status
  kept`, which can never show a kept claim: `Memory.list_entries/1` globs
  `memory/<category>/*.md` and parses v0.65 review-status entries, and a
  claim-stream file is a different format. `memory show <claim path>` returns
  `Memory entry not found`. Bind the claim path from the claim id instead.
- SV-8A.5c is the only step that builds the Memory projection, and it runs long
  after SV-6C asserts retrieval works, so SV-6C cannot pass as ordered. After
  M9.b.10.a the boot path covers it; SV-8A.5c stays as the explicit job row.

Acceptance: SV-6B.5 and SV-6C pass on a fresh Home in the documented order.

### 2026-08-03 — M9.b.10 implemented and verified end to end

**M9.b.10.a.** `Memory.Projection.init/1` now takes `bootstrap_jobs?` and, when it
boots with no serving generation, continues into `:bootstrap_projection`, which
reconciles the managed row and kicks `memory-index-rebuild`. `application.ex`
starts the owner as `{AllbertAssist.Memory.Projection, bootstrap_jobs?: true}`.
This is deliberately the same seam Search already used — Search was started with
`bootstrap_jobs?: true` and Memory with no options at all, which was the entire
asymmetry. Forget recovery kicks the same managed job, so
`maybe_kick_forget_recovery/1` now reports whether it fired and the bootstrap
only runs when it did not, avoiding a double kick. The writer-lock guarantee is
unchanged and still comes from `maybe_add_projection_owners/1`, which adds the
owner only when the lock is held.

**M9.b.10.b.** `ProposalReview.refresh_projection/1` calls
`Projection.queue_repair/2` when `refresh_claim/1` fails, so `repair_pending`
names a repair that is scheduled rather than a word that was recorded and
dropped.

**M9.b.10.c.** New `memory/projection_bootstrap_test.exs` (4 rows) plus one row
in `proposal_review_test.exs`. Both were proven non-vacuous by reverting the
production change and re-running: the bootstrap row fails with "booting a
not-ready projection with bootstrap_jobs?: true must kick memory-index-rebuild",
and the keep row fails on `dirty_seq` with `left: 0`. The new rows register the
owner under its real module name, because every existing row starts it with
`name: nil` — so `Process.whereis(Projection)` is nil and a keep never reaches
`refresh_claim/1` at all. That, plus the explicit `Projection.rebuild/1` calls,
is how the suite masked the defect twice over.

**End-to-end proof on a fresh Home, no manual job run.** Daemon healthy in 12
seconds (SV-2's 90-second bound unaffected); `memory-index-rebuild` moved from a
null `next_due_at` to a scheduled one; the scheduler promoted a generation about
30 seconds after boot; control reported
`state: "ready", current_generation_id: 019fc861-…, dirty: false`; and
`mix allbert admin memory retrieve` returned `No Active Memory chunks retrieved`
— a real answer for an empty Home — where it previously failed with
`** (Mix) Memory command failed: :memory_projection_not_ready`.

**Regression sweep.** 147 tests across `memory`, `search`, and `jobs`: 0
failures. Full `--warnings-as-errors` compile clean.

**Operator-attested, 2026-08-03, daemon 69125 at `5aefbcfc7`.** The operator ran
SV-6B.5 and SV-6C on a fresh Home. The keep recorded
`projection: {"outcome":"refreshed","revision":1}` where the same row previously
recorded `repair_pending` / `:memory_projection_not_ready`, and control moved to
`state: "ready", projection_revision: 1, dirty: false`. SV-6C then returned the
kept claim at `Active Memory chunks: 1` with `lexical=1.0, identity=1.0`, both
implicitly and at explicit `--valid-at`/`--known-at` coordinates, resolving the
same chunk `873a424156647ea0`. The canonical fields were unchanged throughout
(`append_outcome: appended`, `outcome: kept`), confirming the canonical layer was
never at fault. This closes M9.b.10's acceptance on the exact path that failed.
Setup steps above SV-6B.5 were run by the agent, so this is a targeted re-test of
the fix and not an SV pass; the acceptance run remains a full SV-0 through SV-9
on a fresh Home.

**M9.b.10.d.** SV-6B.5 now derives the claim path from the proposal id
(`<ALLBERT_HOME>/memory/notes/<claim_id>.md`) and asserts the claim-stream header
and `"state":"kept"`, instead of binding it from `memory list --status kept`,
which lists a different store entirely. SV-6C carries a note that the projection
is now built at boot, that SV-8A.5c remains the explicit managed-job row, and
that an operator who hits `:memory_projection_not_ready` must wait and record a
failure rather than running a manual rebuild — a hand rebuild would hide exactly
the defect the row now covers.

### M9.b.11 — Memory projection staleness after archive, restore, and Forget (ship blocker)

`[code]` `[test]` `[docs]`

Found by attended SV-6D.3 on 2026-08-03, daemon 69125, immediately after M9.b.10
made retrieval work at all. M9.b.10 fixed the *bootstrap*; this is the *update*
path. A claim that is archived and then restored is canonically `kept` but stays
permanently unretrievable.

**Reproduction.** SV-6D.1/6D.2 archived the kept claim with confirmation, SV-6D.3
restored it. The canonical claim stream is correct and complete —
`seq=1 kept (proposal_keep)`, `seq=2 archived (archive)`, `seq=3 kept (restore)`
— and `memory restore` reported `State: kept`. But
`mix allbert admin memory retrieve` returned `Active Memory chunks: 0`, and the
projection sat at `projection_revision: 1`, `state: "degraded"`,
`last_error_code: "canonical_revalidation_failed"`.

**Defect 1 — archive/restore/Forget never refresh the projection.**
`Projection.refresh_claim/1` is called from exactly one place,
`ProposalReview.refresh_projection/1`. Keeping a proposal advances the projection
revision; archiving, restoring, and forgetting do not. The projection therefore
holds the revision digest from the original keep while canonical has moved on.

**This also reframes SV-6D.2's apparent pass.** Archive returned
`Active Memory chunks: 0`, which is the expected answer, but not because the
archive propagated. The projection was already stale and the canonical recheck
refused to serve it. Right answer, wrong reason. Restore is where the mask slips,
because there the correct answer is non-zero. The recheck itself is behaving
exactly as ADR 0089 specifies and is the reason a stale projection can never
become authoritative; the gap is that nothing repairs it afterwards.

**Defect 2 — `queue_repair` marks dirty without kicking, in the daemon only.**
Reproduced live: one retrieve moved the projection control's `dirty_seq` from 2
to 3 with `last_error_code: canonical_revalidation_failed`, while the
`memory-index-rebuild` job's metadata `dirty_seq` stayed at 1 and `next_due_at`
stayed empty. `Managed.kick/2` never ran. The same
`Projection.queue_repair/2` path was exercised in an isolated test with
`ALLBERT_HOLD_WRITER_LOCK=1` and did kick correctly (job `dirty_seq` 0 → 1), so
the mechanism is right and something specific to the daemon prevents it.

**Not yet proven.** The most likely cause is that `Managed.kick/2` runs
`Repo.transaction(..., mode: :immediate)` from inside the Projection GenServer
and fails against the daemon's single-writer arrangement, since
`handle_info(:kick_projection_repair, …)` records a failure only as an in-memory
diagnostic that no operator surface reports. That is a hypothesis; M0 of this
milestone must confirm it before choosing a fix.

**A silent failure is part of the defect.** `projection_repair_kick_failed` is
recorded in GenServer state and never surfaces in `memory status`, `/health`, or
the daemon log. A repair that cannot be scheduled should be visible.

#### M9.b.11.a — confirm the kick failure mode

`[audit]`

Prove or disprove the transaction hypothesis before changing code. Acceptance: the
exact failure of `Managed.kick/2` inside the daemon's Projection process is
recorded with evidence, not inferred.

#### M9.b.11.b — refresh the projection on every claim transition

`[code]`

Archive, restore, and Forget advance the projection the way a keep does, rather
than leaving it to a repair that may never run. Acceptance: after each
transition the projection revision matches canonical, and retrieval reflects the
new state without a manual rebuild.

#### M9.b.11.c — make a queued repair either run or be visible

`[code]`

Whatever M9.b.11.a finds, a repair that is marked must either be scheduled or
reported. Acceptance: a projection that cannot schedule its own repair says so on
an operator-visible surface.

#### M9.b.11.d — cover the transitions unassisted

`[test]`

Archive → retrieve, restore → retrieve, and Forget → retrieve on a fresh Home
with no manual rebuild, plus a row proving `queue_repair` reaches `Managed.kick`
in a daemon-shaped arrangement. Rows must fail against current code.

#### M9.b.11.e — runbook

`[docs]`

SV-6D.2's note must stop describing the canonical recheck as the archive
propagating, and record that the row passed for the wrong reason until
M9.b.11.b.

### 2026-08-03 — M9.b.11 implemented and verified

**M9.b.11.a — the hypothesis recorded above was wrong, and the record should say
so.** The plan guessed that `Managed.kick/2`'s `Repo.transaction(mode: :immediate)`
was failing inside the Projection GenServer. It was not. The guard never let the
kick run: `WriterLockHolder.enabled?/0` reads the `ALLBERT_HOLD_WRITER_LOCK`
environment variable, and `Mix.Tasks.Allbert.with_source_daemon_env/1` sets it
around startup and restores it in an `after` block. It describes how the daemon
was *launched*, not whether this VM owns the writer when a repair is queued —
which is exactly why the boot-time bootstrap worked while every later
`queue_repair` silently declined to kick.

Confirmed by controlled change rather than inference: the same Home, the same
stale claim, and the same `memory retrieve` left the job's metadata `dirty_seq`
at 1 under the old guard, and moved it to 2 with `next_due_at` set under the new
one.

**M9.b.11.b.** `ClaimLifecycle.transition/4` refreshes the projection for archive
and restore, reporting a `projection` outcome in its result the way a keep does.
Forget was already correct — `Memory.Forget` calls
`Projection.replace_after_forgets/1` — so the milestone's original framing of
"archive, restore and Forget" narrowed to archive and restore once the code was
read.

**M9.b.11.c.** The guard is now `is_pid(Process.whereis(WriterLockHolder))`,
applied to both the repair kick and the boot-time Forget recovery, so ownership
is a fact about this VM rather than a startup-mode variable.

**M9.b.11.d.** Three rows added: archive and restore advancing the projection
with the claim retrievable again and no rebuild (`proposal_review_test.exs`), and
a pair proving a queued repair reaches `Managed.kick` when the holder runs here
and never does when it does not (`projection_bootstrap_test.exs`). Both new
behaviours were proven non-vacuous by reverting the production change and
re-running; each fails against the original code.

**Verified on the attended Home.** Restarting the daemon with the new guard, a
single retrieve on the still-stale claim kicked the rebuild; the projection
promoted a fresh generation about 96 seconds later and
`mix allbert admin memory retrieve` returned `Active Memory chunks: 1` for the
restored claim that had been unretrievable. With M9.b.11.b the same recovery no
longer waits on a rebuild, because the transition advances the projection
directly.

**Regression sweep.** 150 tests across `memory`, `search`, and `jobs`: 0
failures. `--warnings-as-errors` clean, credo clean, manifest reconciles at 4476
rows.

**M9.b.11.e.** SV-6D.2's note is rewritten. It previously explained the surviving
candidate as the canonical recheck working and called the row a pass; it was
passing for the wrong reason, and the note now says so and states the post-fix
expectation (`Candidate chunks after filter: 0`).

**Operator-attested, 2026-08-03, daemon 6601 at `88c36187e`.** The operator ran
SV-6D.1 through SV-6D.5 on a fresh Home. Archive reported
`Candidate chunks before filter: 0` where the pre-fix run reported `1`, so the
transition propagated rather than the recheck masking a stale candidate. SV-6D.3
— the row that previously failed and ended the session — returned
`Active Memory chunks: 1` immediately with no rebuild wait, and its
`grep -Fq "$V13_MEMORY_PATH"` matched now that SV-1 no longer produces a doubled
separator. SV-6D.4 carried all four disclosure clauses, and SV-6D.5 left zero
chunks with a `forgotten` proposal showing `value=nil` and `claim=nil`. Setup
above SV-6D was agent-run, so this is a targeted re-test; the acceptance run
remains a full SV-0 through SV-9.

SV-6E.1 through SV-6E.3 also passed in the same attended session. SV-6E.1
confirmed the Forget disclosure was honest: the claim was gone while the
originating conversation stayed searchable. SV-6E.2 suppressed the deleted
canonical message from Search against an index reporting roughly 30 minutes of
staleness, which is query-time suppression rather than a reindex and is the
ADR 0093 contract. SV-6E.3 removed the assistant-guess thread while the
negative-control thread survived, and the durable thread's message count fell
from 2 to 1 — the deletion touched exactly one message and one thread.

SV-7A.1 through SV-7A.5 passed in the same session. New canonical content became
searchable in **10 seconds** against the frozen 90-second bound. Term, quoted
phrase, and prefix queries under differing order and surface filters all resolved
the same canonical source, and `--order oldest` demonstrably reversed the
ordering rather than being ignored. Three-surface parity is exact: CLI, TUI, and
Web each returned the identical pair `msg_1d8b3387` and `msg_948544d2` for the
same authorized query. The Web visit also flipped `model_disclosure.web` from
`pending` to `acknowledged`, so all three surfaces now hold their own disclosure
record — and Web is the one surface where that disclosure is actually readable,
since it does not take over the screen the way the TUI does.

SV-8A.1 through SV-8A.6 passed. All five managed rows existed exactly once.
Pausing `search-index` suppressed execution without suppressing tracking —
`dirty_seq` rose 16 to 18 while `clean_dirty_seq` held at 16, so the work was
queued rather than dropped — and resuming indexed the paused-period content with
freshness falling to 3.5 seconds. The four remaining managed jobs each completed
through Jobs, including `memory-index-rebuild`, which reported the projection
ready with no stuck recovery state after the session's archive/restore/Forget
churn. SV-8A.6 proved feature disable retains the managed row: the same job id
survived, `Next due` cleared and was restored, `managed_spec_digest` was
unchanged, and `eligibility_epoch` advanced. SV-7B and SV-8B are recorded
UNAVAILABLE — every non-TUI channel reads `enabled=false`,
`credentials=missing`, `identities=0`.

One observation deliberately not over-claimed: SV-8A.5d consolidated 4 sources
and created 0 proposals, which is consistent with Forget blocking exact
re-proposal but does not isolate it, since SV-6E.2 had also deleted the
originating operator message.

### M9.b.12 — First cumulative `release.v13` run and its triage (ship blocker)

`[audit]` `[code]` `[test]` `[docs]`

The gate ran for the first time on 2026-08-03 at `200626ab7` and **failed**, with
30 unique test failures. This is the first authorized execution: plan line 8535
forbids running `release.v13` during M0.a1–M9.b.5 and reserves it for M9.b, so
accumulated red is the expected discovery, not a process lapse.

> **Closed 2026-08-04: `release.v13` is green — 32 of 32 steps passed at
> `1270045b0` on a clean tree, evidence `release-v13-1785810884.json`
> (2026-08-04T03:11:08Z).** It took four cumulative runs, each surfacing a
> different class: run 1, thirty ExUnit failures; run 2, zero failing tests but
> two **non-test** steps red (`credo_strict`, `v121_lane_inventory`) that the
> count of thirty never covered; run 3, one structural deadlock in the ACP
> fixture that isolation cannot reproduce; run 4, green. **No product behaviour
> was changed by any of the triage.** Every failure was a test that had stopped
> asserting its own contract, which is worse than a red test because it reports
> green while guarding nothing. Details in M9.b.12.d.

**Attribution established by worktree comparison, not inference.** A worktree at
`e00fc1ede` (the last commit before any M9.b.10 production code) ran the failing
fan-out suites and produced `68 tests, 7 failures` — byte-identical to HEAD. The
Memory work in M9.b.10 and M9.b.11 causes none of it. Exactly one gate failure
was ours: `format_check` on the two test files added by M9.b.10.c and M9.b.11.d,
fixed at `b1b0491b4`.

**Dominant root — the M9.b.5.2 fixture migration was never completed.** Commit
`195dfcdf6` ("unify central report fixtures") introduced
`TestSupport.FanoutReportFixture`, which supplies a truthful completion event
when terminalizing a child. Files that adopted it pass. Files that still
hand-roll `TerminalTransitions.terminalize_child/4` with an empty or partial
event now fail `:invalid_fanout_report_completion_event` — 7 of the 30 — because
M9.b.5 correctly tightened that contract. The product is right and the tests are
stale; the defect is an unfinished migration, not 25 independent bugs.

Adopted and passing: `tui_subscriptions`, `notify`, `workspace_live`,
`openai_controller`, `persist_attached_fanout_report`. Still hand-rolled and
failing: `fanout_steering`, `delegate_cancel`, `acp_stdio_server`, `fanout_test`,
`allbert_objectives`, `v11_sweep_eval`, `operator_topology_integration`.

**Remaining clusters, not yet root-caused.** Fan-out announcement text in two
`workspace_live` rows; four `TUITest` kickoff-barrier rows failing on
`Ecto.NoResultsError`/`RuntimeError`; one ACP
`:fanout_report_layout_generation_mismatch`; one `fanout_test` budget row where
`invalid_budget_snapshot` now precedes `legacy_unreviewed_children`; and a
non-fan-out group (onboarding ×2, TUI identity bootstrap ×2, V11 sweep ×2, V12
sweep ×1, operator topology ×1, the transaction census).

#### M9.b.12.a — three unpropagated claim writers

`[code]` `[test]`

Auditing every `Claims.append/3` caller after M9.b.11 found three product sites
that advance canonical claim state without telling the projection —
`ClaimConfirmation.confirm_manual/1`, `ClaimConfirmation.confirm_destination/1`,
and `Drafts.Promotion.promote_memory/3`. Each reproduces M9.b.11's failure: the
projection keeps a stale revision digest, the canonical recheck correctly refuses
to serve it, and the claim is unretrievable until a full rebuild.

Propagation belongs at the call sites, after the append returns. It must **not**
move inside `Claims.append/3`: that function wraps its write in
`lock(claim_id, …)` and `Projection.refresh_claim/1` reads back through
`Claims.read/1`, so propagating inside would hold the per-claim lock across a
`GenServer.call` to the projection owner and couple the canonical writer to it —
the wrong trade given v1.1's single-writer contention history.

A structural test must then enumerate `Claims.append/3` callers and require each
to propagate or be explicitly exempt, so the class cannot recur silently. This is
the same shape as the existing `Repo.transaction` census in
`sqlite_topology_test.exs`, which is precisely the guard that caught the census
drift below.

Acceptance: each of the three propagates; the structural test fails when a new
unpropagated caller is added.

**Implemented 2026-08-03.** The propagation moved into one seam,
`AllbertAssist.Memory.ProjectionSync.refresh/1`, rather than a fourth copy of
the same private helper. `ClaimLifecycle` and `ProposalReview` were refactored
onto it and the three missing writers now call it: both `ClaimConfirmation`
entry points via a `sync_projection/1` pipe on the append result, and
`Drafts.Promotion.promote_memory/3` on its outcome map. The seam runs after
`Claims.append/3` returns, for the locking reason recorded above, and its
moduledoc carries that reasoning so it is not "simplified" into the writer
later.

`memory/claim_writer_propagation_test.exs` is the census. It globs the library
for `Claims.append(` callers and requires each to reference `ProjectionSync`,
`replace_after_forget(s)`, or `rebuild` — with one recorded exemption,
`dev_gates/v13_zero_shot_eval.ex`, which rebuilds the whole projection itself
after seeding fixtures. A third row asserts every exemption is still a claim
writer, so the allowlist cannot outlive its subject. Proven non-vacuous by
reverting `drafts/promotion.ex`: the census fails naming exactly that file.

Verified: 104 memory/drafts tests pass, warnings-as-errors clean, credo clean,
format clean, manifest reconciles at 4479 rows.

#### M9.b.12.b — finish the M9.b.5.2 fixture migration

`[test]`

Migrate the seven hand-rolled `terminalize_child/4` call sites onto
`TestSupport.FanoutReportFixture`. Preserve what each row asserts; the goal is a
truthful completion event, not a passing assertion. Any row that cannot be
expressed through the shared fixture is a signal about the contract and must be
raised rather than special-cased.

**Implemented 2026-08-03.** `FanoutReportFixture.complete_child!/2` was private,
which is why every one-off call site hand-rolled `terminalize_child/4`;
completing one child is the operation tests actually need, so it is now public
and returns the terminal step. Nine call sites across seven files migrated onto
it: `fanout_steering`, `delegate_cancel` (3), `acp_stdio_server`,
`allbert_objectives` (2), `v11_sweep_eval`, `operator_topology_integration`,
`objective_live` (2), and `objectives_live`.

A second, unlooked-for pattern surfaced during the migration and was the real
cause of the layout failures: four call sites hand-rolled selection provenance as
`%{fallback_reason: "model_disabled"}` or pinned `layout_version: 1`, neither of
which survives layout v2. They now select through the fixture's
`select_pending!/2` and `select_claim!/2`, which build provenance via
`Report.fallback_provenance/2` and `Report.prepare_synthesis/2`. That fixed the
`:fanout_report_layout_generation_mismatch` failures in `delegate_cancel`,
`acp_stdio_server`, and `allbert_objectives`, which the completion-event story
alone did not explain.

One row needed more than a mechanical swap and is recorded rather than bent:
`objectives_live`'s "refreshes a joined fan-out" waited for the parent to read
`completed` after completing its children, which no longer happens — completing
children joins the parent but leaves it composing, and it reaches `completed`
only once a report is selected. The row now selects one, which is what it was
always asserting about.

Result: 10 of the 30 gate failures fixed, and every
`:invalid_fanout_report_completion_event` and
`:fanout_report_layout_generation_mismatch` failure is gone. Unused
`TerminalTransitions` aliases were dropped from four files. Compile, format, and
strict credo are clean; the manifest reconciles at 4479 rows.

**Deliberately not migrated.** `objectives/fanout_test.exs` already passes
truthful completion events at all four of its call sites; its single failure is
the budget-snapshot root, not this cluster. `dev_gates/v13_zero_shot_eval.ex`
drives the projection itself.

**Remaining after 12.b**, all separate roots for M9.b.12.d: three
`acp_stdio_server` `Task.await` timeouts; four `TUITest` kickoff-barrier rows;
one `fanout_test` budget row where `invalid_budget_snapshot` now precedes
`legacy_unreviewed_children`; one `v11_sweep_eval` row returning
`:needs_confirmation` where `:completed` is expected; two `workspace_live`
announcement-text rows; and the non-fan-out group (onboarding ×2, TUI identity
bootstrap ×2, V12 sweep ×1, transaction census ×1).

#### M9.b.12.c — review the transaction census rather than bump it

`[audit]` `[test]`

`objectives/fanout/terminal_transitions.ex` holds 7 `Repo.transaction(`
callsites against an approved 3. History: 3 at v1.1 M12.15 and M9.b.4, 6 at
M9.b.5, 7 at M9.b.5.2. The census exists (v1.1 M12.21) so new callsites are
reviewed rather than appearing silently, given single-writer contention. Review
the four additions for bounded `mode: :immediate` use, then update the approved
map with that review recorded. Do not simply change 3 to 7.

**Reviewed and updated 2026-08-03.** All seven callsites were read and every one
opens with `mode: :immediate` — the property this census protects, because under
the single-writer topology a deferred transaction that upgrades to a write lock
mid-flight is the `SQLITE_BUSY` shape v1.1 M12.19 was built to avoid. The four
added during M9.b.5/M9.b.5.2 are `transition_active_child`,
`persist_selected_composition`, `rebind_composing_transaction`, and the selection
path at the end of the module; each is a bounded durable transition on the
fan-out terminal path, not a new long-lived writer. The approved map now reads 7
with that review recorded inline, so the next reader sees why rather than a
number moved to make a test pass.

#### M9.b.12.d — root-cause and disposition the remaining clusters

`[audit]` `[code]` `[test]`

Each remaining failure is classified as stale expectation or product defect, with
evidence. Product defects get their own remediation; stale expectations are
updated to the contract M9.b.4/5/6 established.

**The dominant root: fan-out admission now requires model readiness.** M9.b added
`Runtime.fanout_role_readiness_step/3`, which refuses to admit a fan-out when the
`fanout_manager` and `fanout_synthesis` roles resolve to no callable model. Tests
written before that gate never stubbed readiness, so their input fell through to
a single direct answer and they stopped asserting fan-out behaviour at all — a
row named for advisory decomposition was passing judgement on a decomposition
that never happened. This accounted for failures across four separate files
(`channels/tui_test.exs`, `public_protocol/acp_stdio_server_test.exs`,
`security/v11_sweep_eval_test.exs`, `live/workspace_live_test.exs`).

The admission setup is now one shared seam, `test/support/fanout_roles.ex`
(`AllbertAssist.TestSupport.FanoutRoles.configure!/0`), rather than copied into
each file: it installs `Test.ModelReadinessFake` and points `direct_answer`,
`fanout_manager`, and `fanout_synthesis` at a resolvable local profile. It is
admission infrastructure only — it relaxes no assertion, it puts the fan-out into
the state each test already believed it was in.

**A second, distinct root: a disclosure literal a truthfulness fix had removed.**
`v12-enable-provenance-001` asserted `"stays on this device"`. M9.b.3
deliberately reworded that disclosure to "Inference uses your configured local
endpoint", because a local provider may be reachable at a configured endpoint
that is not this machine — the old sentence made a claim Allbert cannot
guarantee. The row's binding is `:disclosure_derived_from_selection`, so it was
rebound to the selection it derives from (profile, provider, and the
local-inference clause, plus a refutation that a local-ready selection discloses
hosted egress) rather than re-pinned to another literal that the next
truthfulness fix would break the same way.

**Measured disposition of the 30.** Every gate step was re-run individually after
M9.b.12.a–c and the fixes above:

| Gate step | First run | After 12.d |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `v11_authority_sweep` | 2 | 0 |
| `v11_runtime_fanout` | 12 | 3 |
| `v11_operator_sqlite_topology` | 2 | 0 |
| `v11_channel_authority` | 4 | 0 |
| `v11_web_operator` | 5 | 0 |
| `v12_authority_sweep` | 1 | 0 |
| `v12_enablement_and_onboarding` | 4 | 3 |
| **Total** | **30** | **6** |

No product change was made for any of these. Every one was a test that had
stopped asserting its own contract — which is the more troubling finding, since
such a test reports green while guarding nothing. The 30 were counted from a
single early run, so several were already closed by M9.b.12.b's fixture
migration before being separately attributed; the table above is measured per
step, not derived by subtraction.

**A third root, and the most serious of the three: the gate is not hermetic.**
`:first_model_state_override` substitutes only the *global starter model* probe.
M9.b.3 qualified DirectAnswer routing per task, and that path reaches
`Settings.ModelDoctor`, which probes the endpoint configured in settings — a live
localhost Ollama that no override intercepts. `OLLAMA_HOST` does not help; that
variable feeds only the global probe.

The consequence is that `CLI.Areas.OnboardingTest`'s `model_path` row passed on
CI and failed on a developer machine running Ollama with a model matching
`direct_answer_local`. Proven directly: on this machine the doctor returned
`model: "qwen2.5:7b", redacted_host: "localhost", model_available: true` — the
real model name off the live server. A release gate whose result depends on
ambient machine state is not a gate, and `mix/tasks/allbert.test.ex` still
carries the now-false claim that "the first-model probe is overridden in the test
env, so no localhost Ollama probe runs".

The row is fixed test-side by pinning `providers.local_ollama.base_url` to an
unreachable port, the convention `workspace_onboarding_test.exs` and
`onboarding_provider_eval_test.exs` already use, so `:model_missing` holds for
the task model too.

That surfaced a second and larger isolation gap in the same file: it set
`ALLBERT_HOME` but never the `Settings` root, so it inherited whatever root
another file had put in app env. The same row therefore reported the DirectAnswer
route unavailable, the starter model missing, or the model ready, purely
according to what ran before it — it was order-dependent in three directions.
The file now owns its `Settings` root and clears `Settings.Fragments` on both
edges of the setup.

With both fixed, the row's **original assertions hold unchanged** in isolation
and after other files: no local or hosted profile is usable, readiness falls back
to the substrate, and the wizard says "The runtime is up, but the starter model
isn't downloaded." An interim commit had rewritten those assertions to the
route-qualified wording; that was compensating for the leak rather than fixing
it, and has been reverted. Nothing in this row asserts less than it did before.

**Recorded because it was nearly a false diagnosis.** This was first read as a
product defect: a first-run dead end in which the wizard tells an operator with
no starter model "Your model is ready." The `EnablementTest` rows refused the
corresponding fix and were right to — `:model_missing` names the *global* starter
model, and a qualified task model may be healthy while the global 3B is absent
(`"an already-enabled qualified task remains ready when the global 3B is
absent"`). M9.b.3's reading of `direct_answer_readiness` at `model_path` is
correct. No product change was made; the only surviving `lib` edit is a comment
in `Enablement.selection_for/4` recording why `model_state` deliberately does not
gate that clause.

**Two non-test gate steps also failed and were not part of the thirty**, which
counted ExUnit failures only:

- `credo_strict` — two nested-module calls introduced by the hermeticity fix
  itself. Aliased.
- `v121_lane_inventory` — five files whose declared `@moduletag` no longer
  matched the lane the inventory derives. Three (`operator_read_actions_test`,
  `fanout_manager_test`, `model_readiness_test`) were failing in the **first**
  gate run too and are v1.3's own drift: all three were last touched by M9.b.4.3
  and M9.b.6, which added the `Req.Test` stubs and `System.put_env` calls that
  moved them. They are retagged to the derived lane, matching house convention —
  non-eval `Req.Test` files are `:external_runtime_serial` elsewhere
  (`allbert_skills_test`, `finder_test`). The other two are M9.b.13's new Memory
  files, which tripped the `db` classifier on an incidental `Ecto.UUID.generate/0`
  used only for unique ids and a `Repo.transaction` mention inside a moduledoc;
  both were rewritten so the files genuinely match the `:pure_async` lane they
  declare, rather than adjudicated around. `docs/validation/test-manifest.csv`
  regenerated: 63 rows re-laned, 4,539 total unchanged.

**A fourth root, found only by the cumulative run: fixture timing assumptions
that hold only on an idle machine.** Gate run 3 failed one ACP row with an
ExUnit 60s `TimeoutError` in a file that passes 3/3 in isolation. The budgets
were ordered backwards: `session/prompt` blocks in `Runtime.await_fanout/3` for
up to `fanout_continuation_timeout_ms` (120s by default) waiting for a report to
be selected, and the test fixture is what selects it — but the fixture polled for
only 500 × 10ms = 5s. Any load pushing child completion past five seconds
deadlocked the row: the selector was already gone, nothing could ever join, and
the test died on ExUnit's timeout rather than its own assertion. The poll budget
must outlive the hold it services, so it is now 45s with `Task.await` at 50s,
both inside the 60s test timeout so a genuine "never queued" still reports
`:composition_not_queued` instead of hanging. This also explains an earlier
5s `Task.await` failure that had been written off as ACP flakiness — same root.

**The last intermittent row, diagnosed rather than tolerated.** The
`StoreTuiIdentityBootstrapTest` concurrent-launch row was failing roughly one run
in nineteen. Running it under deliberate CPU contention — the condition the
cumulative gate creates and an idle isolated run never does — reproduced it on
the third attempt, and the tally the previous commit added named the cause on
first sight:

```
%{{:error, {:settings_lock_timeout, "database is locked"}} => 9,
  {:ok, :bootstrapped} => 1, {:ok, :present} => 2}
```

`StoreLock.with_lock/2` gives up after 5s. Twelve racers contending for one
exclusive SQLite lock do not all clear that budget on a loaded machine, so nine
returned `:settings_lock_timeout`. A bounded lock timeout is designed behaviour
and twelve simultaneous first launches is not a real scenario — the timeout is
not the defect. **The assertion was**: the row demanded that all twelve *win a
lock race within five seconds*, a timing property, on top of the convergence
property its name describes.

It now asserts what must hold under any interleaving — exactly one bootstrap,
exactly one audit row, no `:activated` disposition (which would mean a torn write
of the bootstrap subset), and no failure other than a lock timeout — and then
proves convergence **deterministically**, by launching again after the burst has
settled and requiring `:present` with `written: []` and the one default mapping.
That is strictly stronger than the count it replaced on every property that
matters, and it no longer depends on scheduler luck. Verified 0 failures in 12
runs under the same load that reproduced the flake.

All three intermittent-looking failures in this milestone had concrete structural
causes. None was irreducible.

### M9.b.13 — Memory coverage floor (ship blocker)

`[test]` `[docs]`

**Operator scope decision, 2026-08-03: v1.3 ships the full Memory surface, with
targeted coverage added for the modules that have none.** The alternatives —
cutting the surface to what attended validation exercised, shipping unguarded, or
deferring Memory to its own release — were weighed and declined. Memory is v1.3's
headline capability and the defect class behind every finding is now closed and
guarded; the remaining exposure is fragility, not known breakage.

**What the decision rests on.** Five product defects were found in Memory during
M9.b: the missing projection bootstrap (M9.b.10), archive and restore not
propagating plus the repair guard reading a startup environment variable
(M9.b.11), and three further unpropagated claim writers (M9.b.12.a). All five
reduce to one root — the projection's consistency with canonical state depended
on explicit call sites and the set was incomplete — which `Memory.ProjectionSync`
and the `claim_writer_propagation_test` census now close.

**The uncomfortable part, recorded honestly.** Not one of the five was caught by
a test. Four surfaced in attended operator validation and three in the audit that
followed; before the census the suite's ability to catch this class was zero.
Seven of the twenty-four Memory modules have no direct test reference, and
`ClaimConfirmation` — one of the seven — is exactly where the audit found an
unpropagated writer. That correlation is the argument for this milestone.

**Measured, not assumed.** `SecretFilter` is the highest-stakes untested module:
it is the only thing keeping credential-shaped content out of Memory, and only
its `sk-` pattern is asserted anywhere in the repo — `ghp_` and `AKIA` have zero
references. Every pattern was exercised directly on 2026-08-03 and all six
matched correctly, with a benign control refused. The risk there is regression,
not a live defect.

**Attended validation covered roughly eleven of the twenty Memory subcommands**
— consolidate, proposals, proposal, proposal-review, retrieve, archive, restore,
forget, list, show, status. Not exercised: `compile-index`, `delete`,
`promote-turn`, `proposal-keep-all`, `prune`, `review`, `search`, `summarize`,
`update`. Shipping the full surface means these rest on unit coverage rather than
operator evidence, which is what this milestone supplies.

#### M9.b.13.a — coverage for the seven untested modules

`[test]`

Direct rows for `ClaimConfirmation`, `CollectionPolicy`, `ConsolidationControl`,
`Lexical`, `Retrieval`, `ReviewCadence`, and `SecretFilter`.

`SecretFilter` must assert every pattern it declares, including `ghp_` and
`AKIA`, plus a benign control that is not refused — a filter that refuses
everything is as broken as one that refuses nothing, and only the negative
control distinguishes them.

`ClaimConfirmation` must cover both entry points including the projection
propagation added in M9.b.12.a, since that is the module the audit caught.

Acceptance: each of the seven has direct rows; no row passes against a
deliberately broken implementation of the behaviour it claims to cover.

**Implemented 2026-08-03. Zero Memory modules now have no direct test
reference**, down from seven.

`SecretFilter` (16 rows) asserts every declared shape including all five GitHub
prefixes and both AWS forms, each paired with a negative control. `Lexical` (8),
`ReviewCadence` (6), `Retrieval` (7), `CollectionPolicy` (12), and
`ConsolidationControl` (8) cover their own boundaries. `ClaimConfirmation` turned
out to be exercised end to end through both confirmation actions already — the
"zero references" figure was a naming artifact — but **no row asserted the
projection outcome M9.b.12.a added**, and `ApproveConfirmation` does not surface
the target action's result, so it cannot be asserted from there. Three rows now
call the module directly; the propagation row was proven non-vacuous by reverting
`claim_confirmation.ex` to its pre-M9.b.12.a state, where it fails
`KeyError: key :projection not found`.

**Four findings recorded rather than smoothed over.**

`SecretFilter`'s labelled-assignment lookahead intends to exempt `secret://`, but
the exemption is defeated: `secret` is itself one of the alternation keywords, so
the scanner re-anchors on the literal `secret:` inside the value. The rows assert
what it does, because the deviation fails **closed** — it refuses a secret
*reference*, which is not a secret — and loosening a credential filter for a
cosmetic intent is the wrong trade.

`ConsolidationControl` declares `unique_constraint/3` with the correct index
name, but under this adapter a scope-tuple violation is **not** converted into a
changeset error; it raises `Ecto.ConstraintError`. Asserted as it behaves. A
caller written against `{:error, changeset}` would crash instead, which is worth
knowing before one is written.

`ConsolidationControl` also marks `e2ee`, `run_sequence`, and `last_run` as
`validate_required` while each carries a schema default, so omitting them is
valid. "Required" reads stronger than it behaves; recorded so the next reader is
not misled.

`CollectionPolicy.reauthorize_evidence/2` treats an evidence map without a
`refs` list as one inline ref, so an empty map is a malformed ref rather than
zero refs and fails closed on origin scope. That is the right direction —
rehydrating nothing and reporting success would let a proposal claim provenance
it never had.

**Three of my own expectations were wrong and corrected against the code, not the
reverse**: `at` is not a stop word, `markdown_search/2` returns `entries` rather
than `chunks`, and fields with schema defaults satisfy `validate_required`.

**Push protection.** The first attempt was rejected by GitHub secret scanning on
the Slack fixture. The offered remedy was to allowlist the fake secret, which
normalises bypassing push protection for the sake of a test. The fixtures are
assembled at runtime from fragments instead; verified with the filter's own
patterns that no literal remains in the file.

Verified: 154 memory tests pass, compile/format/strict-credo clean.

#### M9.b.13.b — record what rests on unit coverage rather than operator evidence

`[docs]`

The nine unexercised subcommands are named in the release evidence as covered by
tests rather than by attended validation. This is a disclosure, not a caveat to
bury: an operator reading the evidence should be able to tell which parts of
Memory a human actually drove.

**Implemented 2026-08-03** as `docs/validation/v1.3/README.md`. It records the
eleven subcommands attended validation drove with their SV rows and results, the
nine that rest on unit coverage with where each is covered, and the known
deviations with their direction of failure — a deviation that fails closed being
a different risk from one that fails open. It also states plainly that raising
coverage closes the class going forward without retroactively turning unit
coverage into operator evidence, and declines to claim a green release gate until
one exists.

### M9.b.14 — Homebrew installed-evidence mode preservation (K5 ship blocker)

`[code]` `[test]` `[external]` `[docs]`

K5 generation `v13-20260804T062430Z-bfddd32013c4` proved all three native
builders at clean pushed SHA `bfddd3201`: each archive passed its in-builder
package/license/runtime smoke, the Linux rows used the same pinned native
multi-architecture digest, and the local directory contained the exact twelve
SHA/generation-bound target files. The generation nevertheless failed before
staging. Under K5's required operator `umask 077`, Homebrew copied the four
sealed root evidence files from archive mode `0644` to installed mode `0600`.
`brew test` then correctly stopped at `THIRD-PARTY-MANIFEST.json` with
`generated_file_corrupt`; the archive viewer had passed, so this is specifically
an installed-package boundary defect. The unpublished draft, provisional tag,
all target files, and temporary formula are discarded as one failed generation,
and published `1.2.6` is restored before remediation.

Remediation stays in the formula seam. Extend its one bounded preservation set
from the two known relocation-managed Mach-O payloads to those two payloads plus
the sealed release evidence roots (`LICENSE`, `NOTICE`,
`THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.md`, `THIRD-PARTY-MANIFEST.json`, and `licenses/`). Restore
that archive in `post_install` with archived permissions explicitly preserved.

The first implementation of that plan was necessary but insufficient. K5
generation `v13-20260804T065148Z-a029d4378ab6` again built all three targets at
one clean pushed SHA, but its Homebrew row proved that the URL archive had
already been unpacked under `umask 077` before the formula's `install` method
ran. The private preservation archive therefore contained `0600` entries for
both `THIRD-PARTY-MANIFEST.json` and `licenses/catalog.json`; `tar -xpzf` then
correctly restored the wrong archived modes. The unpublished generation is
discarded whole and published `1.2.6` is restored again.

The corrected remediation owns both sides of the formula boundary: before
creating the private archive, canonicalize only the bounded sealed evidence
regular files to `0644` and directories to `0755`, then restore that archive in
`post_install` with archived permissions preserved. The two Mach-O payloads
remain byte-preserved by the same archive, without broad chmod over the release.
This makes the packaged evidence contract independent of both the invoking
umask and Homebrew's initial extraction mode. Do not weaken the license verifier,
infer permissions from mutable host defaults, interpret the license manifest in
Homebrew, or add a license-management process.

Focused acceptance is red/green at the owning install-path test: the formula
names exactly the bounded payload set, canonicalizes evidence before archiving,
uses permission-preserving extraction, and a disposable round trip beginning
with Homebrew-style `0600`/`0700` source modes retains `0644`/`0755` evidence
under `umask 077`. Prove the corrected formula with that disposable
Homebrew-extraction fixture before another release generation.
Run the install-path suite, license suite, focused formatter,
warnings-as-errors compile, targeted strict Credo, docs gate, and diff hygiene.
Then commit and push the remediation before constructing a wholly new
three-target generation. K5 repeats all three builders because the source SHA
and artifact bytes change; the Homebrew row must pass `brew test`, installed
`allbert licenses --json`, exact installed evidence modes, and the two exact
Mach-O hash comparisons before staging. No aggregate gate runs for this finding;
K6 remains the single final aggregate rejoin after packaged operator acceptance.

**Implemented and externally proven 2026-08-04.** Pushed SHA `a3cd57816`
canonicalizes only the bounded sealed evidence files/directories before the
formula's preservation archive. The focused install/license suite passed
`36/36`; Ruby syntax, formatter, warnings-as-errors compile, targeted strict
Credo, deterministic license verification, docs gate, and diff hygiene passed.
A disposable real Homebrew install under `umask 077` passed before release
generation. Complete generation `v13-20260804T071638Z-a3cd5781617b` then built
all three native archives from full SHA
`a3cd5781617b3a9ee511425b687ff359bdfcb0c7`: macOS arm64
`8400ae64b991cc97d428dd058a21dcfb5eff5a2379f1b3deb363055e3ec10588`, Linux
x64 `7adf07cf095ed8c82bff69bf59aef79be9d854a4c087bbefb61cead24814dd55`, and
Linux arm64 `8d499eb39885150e9471485e3deb1282a244844bfc28ce637a09e0a4ee57072f`.
The actual candidate Homebrew row passed `brew test`, packaged license
verification, exact `0644`/`0755` evidence modes, and both Mach-O hash
comparisons. Draft release `364678592` staged exactly thirteen assets and
no-build qualification run `30888023502` passed all targets. The four exact
packaged Memory/Search host cells are now separately recorded in the metrics
store, satisfying ADR 0092's final acceptance condition. No aggregate gate ran.

### M9.b.15 — Packaged operator-validation contract reconciliation

`[docs]` `[external]`

The first PV-0 through PV-8 attempt against qualified generation
`v13-20260804T071638Z-a3cd5781617b` passed PV-0 through PV-4 and the executable
parts of PV-5/PV-6, then stopped before any Memory or Search write. It exposed
two request-flow defects rather than release-byte defects:

- PV-5 claimed that the DirectAnswer disclosure and attach banner were the
  first two lines. The installed client correctly rendered the DirectAnswer,
  fan-out manager, and fan-out synthesis disclosures before attaching. That is
  the intentional route set asserted by `first_run/disclosure_test.exs`; the
  source and packaged literals had drifted behind it.
- PV-7 treated the already-dispositioned acknowledgment-to-commitment mode as a
  new hard stop. That contradicts M9.b.8 and SV-6A.1a, which require the exact
  known mode to be recorded and permit validation to continue while every
  other routing or semantic failure remains blocking. The packaged head
  reproduced the disclosed future-commitment wording, so the operator correctly
  closed through PV-8 instead of entering the explicit Memory write.

Remediation is documentation-only and systemic across both source and packaged
instructions: enumerate the three configured route disclosures before attach;
make PV-7 use the same closed known-mode adjudication as SV-6A.1a; and make its
PASS declaration describe the boundary and explicit Memory behavior without

**Pre-closeout handover reconciliation, 2026-08-04.** Two attended-source
findings are confirmed but do not reopen the frozen 1.3.0 candidate. First,
`Memory.Projection.refresh_canonical_claim/2` advances
`projection_revision` after an incremental keep/archive/restore but does not
recompute `claim_stream_watermark`; only a full build writes that field. The
field is currently write-only, verification ignores it, and a full rebuild
self-heals it, so no shipped read, authority, recovery, or drift decision is
wrong. Until a follow-up chooses either live incremental recomputation or an
honest last-full-build name/contract, no drift detector may consume it.
Second, `Jobs.Managed.apply_kick/1` preserves a paused job's status and run
admission remains closed, but for an enabled managed feature it can repopulate
`next_due_at`; the operator surface therefore presents a schedule timestamp as
if it were a run commitment. Follow-up should keep `next_due_at` nil while
paused (preferred) or relabel the field explicitly as a schedule occurrence,
with pause -> dirty kick -> show -> resume coverage.

**Final K6 `release.v13` diagnostic, 2026-08-04.** The exact candidate run
completed 31/32. Its sole red step was `v11_runtime_fanout`, seed `161591`:
`Budget v1 recovery closes blocked and pending-steered DirectAnswer states
once` reached the correct two terminal child states but its injected recovery
transaction hook recorded zero calls instead of two per child. All later
inherited and v1.3 steps passed. Evidence is
`/var/folders/nc/r_scv0hd78x07x908ymg5mk80000gn/T/allbert_test_gates/release-v13/p0-14339/home/release_evidence/v13/release-v13-1785869464.json`.
The exact row passed alone at the same seed.

The failure is a test-control race, not evidence of a production transition
failure. `Steering.steer/3` synchronously asks the global Scheduler to wake the
parent; that call queues a self-message for recovery. The test records two
pending steering directives before kickoff acknowledgement, then acknowledges
and immediately requests a coordinator with an injected hook. Under the loaded
gate, a queued wake was consumed after acknowledgement and the default
coordinator correctly closed both historical children before the injected
coordinator could own recovery. The terminal state was right; the test's
private call counter therefore stayed at zero.

Remediate only the fixture contract: after recording pending steering and
while kickoff is still unacknowledged, cross a synchronous Scheduler mailbox
barrier and prove no coordinator owns the parent; only then acknowledge and
start the explicitly injected coordinator. This makes the wake-up hint's
ordering explicit without changing Scheduler, Steering, lifecycle, or product
behavior. Prove the exact row repeatedly at seed `161591`, the complete
Supervision owner file at that seed, and the exact `v11_runtime_fanout` step,
then run formatter, warnings-as-errors compile, docs, and diff hygiene. Do not
run an aggregate per fix. Because even a test-only tracked commit changes the
release source identity, replace the unpublished generation whole, rerun the
required package identity/integrity/qualification/latency rows, inherit the
accepted PV feature observations without relabelling them, and perform one
fresh final structure -> `release.v13` -> authoritative `release` rejoin.

### M9.b.17 — StockSage descriptor-owned operator-language reconciliation

`[plugin]` `[test]` `[docs]`

The exact-candidate authoritative aggregate completed every phase before
StockSage and then stopped at `stocksage_tests`: `198` tests ran at seed
`429706`, with two failures in
`plugins/stocksage/test/stocksage/actions/actions_test.exs`. The complete owner
file reproduced the same two failures (`23` tests, `2` failures) at the same
seed in the detached candidate checkout. This is deterministic focused
evidence, not aggregate-load contamination.

Both findings are symptoms of one operator-language regression. The public
selected-candidate projection correctly becomes the executable `:action`
after app-owned `:app_intent` recognition produces a supported registered-
action decision; before remediation, `list my recent analyses` instead left an
unexecuted `:app_intent` selected for clarification. The second prompt,
`list my analyses`, fell through to DirectAnswer instead of running
`list_analyses`. Both literals remain in the public `allbert.ask` usage and the
accepted StockSage operator story, but the StockSage package descriptor
declares only `list analyses`/`recent analyses`; the central safety matcher
therefore correctly refuses to infer authority from either undeclared phrase.

Remediate at the additive package-owned descriptor seam. Declare the two
long-standing operator acts `list my analyses` and `list my recent analyses`
for `list_analyses`; do not add a prompt-specific regex, discard possessive
tokens globally, loosen leading-operator-act evidence, or let active-app
affinity grant execution authority. Preserve both representation layers in the
owner test: descriptor recognition is `candidate_kind: :app_intent`, while the
selected public executable candidate is the registered `:action`. Also prove
the exact descriptor id, registered action decision, and execution through
`Actions.Runner`. Add package-owner tests that exercise
both natural variants plus polite scaffolding and reject supplied/quoted uses
of the same words. This keeps recognition as inert app data while Security
Central and the registered Jido action remain the only effect boundary.

Run the complete StockSage descriptor and actions owners at seed `429706`, the
central descriptor/selection-policy owners that protect the supplied-text
boundary, and focused CLI usage coverage, followed by warning-free test and
development compile, formatter, scoped strict Credo, docs, and diff hygiene.
Do not run an aggregate per fix. Because the descriptor is packaged production
metadata, the tracked correction changes executable candidate bytes: after the
bounded tranche is green and pushed, discard/replace the unpublished
three-target generation as one unit and repeat exact identity, integrity,
package smoke, qualification, and only the packaged/operator rows invalidated
by this routing change. The accepted v1.3 Memory/Search/fan-out PV rows remain
inherited without relabelling because none exercises StockSage. Run one final
K6 structure -> `release.v13` -> authoritative `release` rejoin after the
replacement; promotion remains prohibited.

**Focused closure, 2026-08-05.** The production change adds only the two
operator phrases above to StockSage's inert `list_analyses` descriptor. No
central matcher, permission, action, runner, or active-app rule changed. The
first focused attempt usefully corrected the preliminary classification:
after valid descriptor evidence, `trace_metadata.candidate_kind` remains
`:app_intent` while `intent_candidates.selected` correctly projects the
executable registered `:action`; the original action-kind assertion was not
stale. The owner now proves both layers and the exact descriptor/action ids.

At aggregate seed `429706`, the descriptor/actions owners passed `26/26`; the
central Descriptor, SelectionPolicy, and CLI owners passed `41/41`; and the
complete StockSage phase passed `201/201` (the former `198` plus three new
descriptor-contract tests). The new tests prove every declared StockSage
example/synonym supplies actionable descriptor evidence, both restored natural
variants and polite scaffolding select `list_analyses`, and quoted/supplied/
explanatory uses of those words remain non-authoritative. Test and development
warnings-as-errors compiles are clean, whole formatter check passes, and strict
Credo over the three production/test files reports zero issues across `52`
modules/functions. Docs and diff hygiene close with the implementation commit.
This focused GREEN is remediation evidence, not candidate acceptance: replace
the complete unpublished generation and perform the bounded package/K6 rejoin
before promotion.

`868f354ce9479b8bcef66f15eef104c35b391d5b`, generation
`v13-20260805T090448Z-868f354ce947`, and draft `365400371` passed all three
native builds, exact-archive Homebrew reinstall/test and installed-tree
integrity, complete 13-asset staging, and no-build qualification run
`30992415157`. Promotion was skipped. A focused packaged StockSage row then
proved that `list my analyses` still reached DirectAnswer through the real
Runtime and production two-stage router. The daemon recorded
`selection_not_in_shortlist`; therefore this whole candidate and its successful
qualification are diagnostic only and may not be promoted or partially reused.

The descriptor repair above is correct but exposed a deeper context-loss at the
shared router index. `DescriptorResolver` removes every
`routable_by_default?: false` descriptor while building the production index,
before the already-validated `active_app` on the Runtime request can scope that
turn. Deterministic Engine/IntentAgent tests bypassed that production
availability path, which is why they passed. Repair the shared seam, not
StockSage or a prompt: build the router index with globally routable descriptors
and non-default app descriptors as inert indexed data, then filter entries on
each request to the union of globally routable descriptors and descriptors
owned by that request's validated active app before ranking. Operator-disabled
overrides and capability-gated-off descriptors remain excluded. Active-app
scope changes candidate visibility only; descriptor selection evidence,
constrained disambiguation, the registered action, Security Central, and
confirmation remain the authority/effect chain.
